# LOG

Backward-looking. Newest blocks on top. See `ROADMAP.md` for what's
ahead, `SPEC.md` for the full project spec. Process docs at
`@~/.claude/cc-process.md`.

## 2026-05-11 — Block 1B': Animation visual model exploration ✅ done — Five rev iterations on `web/preview/flat-cube-animated.html` converged on a ribbon-extension visual model the user accepts: single-direction strip slides with off-net source extensions for U/D/R/L moves, rotation primitive for the 9-sticker face turn AND for the 12-sticker F/B edge rings, inverse-opacity dimming behind a default-OFF debug toggle, cross-silhouette clip applied by default so the production view ships clipped. Original 1B plan's P1–P4 (TSX port + e2e) resume in a successor block.
**Goal:** Nail the 2D move-animation visual model in a focused preview-only block, bypassing the iterate-fast cadence that failed in paused block 1B (4 P0 revs in one block without convergence). Output: `web/preview/flat-cube-animated.html` showing all 12 QTM moves animating from solved with a model the user accepts on eyeball; once locked, the original `plans/m9-per-card-animations.md` resumes at P1.
**Milestone:** Successor to paused block 1B on M9.2 ([roadmap entry](ROADMAP.md#milestones), [original 1B plan](plans/m9-per-card-animations.md)). On branch `m9-per-card-animations` continuing from the paused-handoff merge commit `3492161` (which brought rev1–rev4 into main but left the branch alive).
**Approach:** Step back to design-discussion mode before touching code. Inventoried SVG animation primitives, surveyed alternatives (2D-only, 3D-leaning, highlight-only, etc.), landed on "tall ribbon with off-net source extension": treat the affected slice as a rigid group that slides in one direction, with the off-net portion of the cycle pre-positioned outside the cross silhouette and clipped (or shown for debug). Then 5 rev iterations on the preview, each = one atomic commit + user eyeball gate.
**Next:** Block closed. Visual model locked. Resume original 1B plan at P1 (extract math to `web/src/components/cubeAnimation.ts` + extend `FlatCubeRenderer.tsx` with `animation?` prop + vitest) in a fresh successor block whenever ready.
**In progress:**
- **Discussion phase.** Inventoried primitives (translate/rotate groups, clipPath, opacity, z-order, rAF/CSS/WAAPI drivers). Walked through three framings: (A) "whole strip slides as one unit, B virtually extended below D" — user's intuition, the winner; (B) per-sticker independent slides — rejected as visually busy with no canonical direction at the wrap; (C) hybrid 3D-rotation-projected-to-2D — overengineered. Locked the model split: 9-sticker face turn = rotation primitive; 12-sticker linear edges (U/D/R/L) = slide with virtual extension off-net; 12-sticker ring edges (F/B) = rotation primitive around F-center (since F/B cycles form closed rings around F-center, no linear axis to slide along).
- **rev5.0 (`2d42816`).** Full rewrite of `web/preview/flat-cube-animated.html`. 12 panels of solved state in light + dark theme rows. Per panel: SVG sized 17×14 sticker units to accommodate extensions, concurrent animation groups (static-33, ribbon-slide with extensions, face-rotation, F/B ring-rotation when applicable), inverse-opacity overlay dimming outside cross. Slide direction derived data-first from FACELET_MOVES via mode-of-motion-vector across edge stickers (caught my brief's U/D directions being backwards — agent followed the data, got the right answer). Per-panel Play/Reset + global Play All / Reset All. 600 ms forward ease-out, 200 ms reverse ease-out. Mathematically verified all 12 panels: post-progress=1 colors match `applyMove(SOLVED, move)`. 807 lines.
- **rev5.1 (`091ae3a`).** Eyeball: rotation centers off — face turn + F/B ring rotated around the top-left of the center sticker (`col*3 + 1`) instead of the geometric center (`col*3 + 1.5`). Added `faceCenterPx` helper at SVG rotate pivot; kept `faceCenterXY` integer coords for angle math since the (-0.5, -0.5) shift cancels across src/dst/center in atan2. Plus auto-reset-on-play: if progress > 0.99 when Play clicked, runs a reverse leg first, then forward. Per-panel + Play All. +44 lines to 851.
- **rev5.2 (`371e4d1`).** Eyeball: R/R' secondary B-column ribbon was sliding wrong direction — user wants white (U) to come DOWN from above B for R. Fix: secondary slides OPPOSITE primary direction for R/R'/L/L' (extension placement flipped accordingly). Verified post-states: R → B-left=WWW, R' → B-left=YYY, L → B-right=YYY, L' → B-right=WWW. Plus z-order: face-rotation and F/B ring-rotation groups now render ABOVE the dimming overlay so mid-rotation swing through cross corners isn't dimmed. Plus mask toggle (default ON at this stage). +103 lines to 954.
- **rev5.3 (`7d6ef22`).** User: invert the mask concept — rename to "Show extensions", default OFF, debug-tool framing. Wrapped ribbon-slide groups in `<g clip-path="url(#extension-clip-…)">` containers (cross silhouette as clipPath). Default = clip applied + overlay hidden = production-shape preview. Debug ON = clip stripped + overlay shown = extensions visible outside cross with dimming. Face/ring groups stay unclipped regardless. Renamed all `cross-mask` / `outside-cross-mask` identifiers → `extension-*`. Pulled timings to named constants: `DUR_FORWARD_MS=600`, `DUR_REVERSE_MS=150` (down from 200), `DUR_PAUSE_MS=250`. Added 250 ms beat between auto-reset and forward (new `legPhase: 'pause'`). +75 lines to 1029.
- **rev5.4 (`3dc7c25`).** Eyeball: thin boundary-sticker strokes — clipPath cut at geometric cross silhouette but sticker strokes (centered, ~0.25px overshoot per side) had their outer half clipped at the boundary. Fix: expanded each clipPath rect by 2px on all sides. 10× the stroke overshoot, 10× smaller than a sticker-width so extensions still fully clip. 2-line change to the two rects in the clipPath definition.
- **Lingering minor artifact, not fixed:** subtle subpixel rendering at some clip-path / sticker-stroke interactions remains visible occasionally at preview pixel scale. Likely browser anti-aliasing territory; production card sizing may not reproduce. Logged as a non-blocker followup.

**Outcome:**
- **Block goal met.** A locked-in visual model for 2D move animation, eyeball-validated across 5 rev iterations. The model:
  - **9-sticker face turn** = rotation primitive around face geometric center (col*3 + 1.5, row*3 + 1.5 in sticker units).
  - **12-sticker edge cycle for U/D/R/L** = linear ribbon slide with off-net virtual extension. U/D = one 5-faces-wide horizontal ribbon at row 3/5. R/L = 4-faces-tall primary at col 5/3 + 2-faces-tall secondary at col 9/11. All ribbons in same move slide in the same general rotation sense; **secondary R/L ribbons slide OPPOSITE primary** because their wrap-source content arrives over the top/bottom of the cube, not from below/above the primary strip.
  - **12-sticker edge cycle for F/B** = rotation primitive around F-center (inner ring radius 3 for F, outer ring radius 4 for B; both rotate around the same center = cross geometric center).
  - **Default view = production-clipped.** Cross silhouette clipPath hides extensions; debug toggle "Show extensions" opt-in to see them outside the cross with dimming.
  - **Timing.** 600 ms forward ease-out, 150 ms reverse ease-out, 250 ms pause between auto-reset and forward when Play is hit on a settled panel.

- **Decisions worth recording (load-bearing for the P1+ port):**
  - **Ribbon-extension framing beats per-sticker projection.** rev1–rev4 in the paused block tried to derive flow direction per sticker via source-projection rules. That framing collapses on the wrap cases (B-face for R/L) because the 2D net's broken topology gives no canonical per-sticker direction. Treating the slice as a rigid ribbon with virtual off-net extension SIDESTEPS the per-sticker direction question — the slide direction is determined by the move's rotation sense and propagates uniformly. Off-net portions are out-of-frame for the clipped view.
  - **F/B = ring rotation, not strip slide.** F/B edge cycles are closed rings around F-center (no linear axis). Use rotation primitive at a wider radius — same primitive as the 9-sticker face turn. No extension needed since the cycle is fully internal to the cross.
  - **Secondary ribbon slides OPPOSITE primary** for R/R'/L/L'. Physical justification: for R, U-right's content goes "over the top of the cube" to land on B-left; projected onto the front-of-cube net view, that content appears to arrive at B from ABOVE B's face. So secondary extension sits above B and slides DOWN, opposite primary's UP. Mirror for R'/L/L'.
  - **Data-first slide direction.** Compute slide direction as the mode of `(post_xy − pre_xy)` across edge stickers, filtering for magnitude-3 vectors. This catches direction errors in author briefs (mine had U/D backwards at rev5.0 — agent followed data, got it right).
  - **Z-order: static + ribbons UNDER overlay; face + ring rotations ABOVE.** Dimming overlay (when debug toggle ON) ghosts off-net extensions but never dims a sticker mid-rotation through a cross-corner region.
  - **Production-clipped by default, extensions as debug toggle.** rev5.0–5.2 had extensions visible by default. rev5.3 flipped this: production view is canonical-cross-clipped clean; extensions only on user opt-in. Matches how the eventual card will render in `SolutionCard.tsx`.
  - **Auto-reset needs a beat.** When Play is hit on settled panel: 150 ms reverse + 250 ms pause + 600 ms forward. Without the pause, reverse-then-forward reads as one jittery motion; with the pause, it reads as "wind back, then play."

- **Followups (in this branch's wake):**
  - **Resume original 1B plan at P1.** `plans/m9-per-card-animations.md` P1 (extract math into `web/src/components/cubeAnimation.ts`, extend `FlatCubeRenderer.tsx` with optional `animation?` prop, vitest contract per move). P2 (TwistyPlayerWrapper play-once + replay ref). P3 (SolutionCard wires both via IntersectionObserver). P4 (e2e refresh). Plan needs minor updates to reflect rev5's visual model (secondary-opposite direction + ring rotation for F/B), but the phase structure stands.
  - **Subpixel border artifact** at clip-path boundaries on rare panels. Revisit only if seen at production card sizing.
  - **Resolved naming question** (discussion): the 2D cube layout is the "**net**" (mathematically; "Latin cross net" specifically). Codebase uses "Flat" but "net" matches cubing.js vocabulary. Optional rename `FlatCubeRenderer` → `NetCubeRenderer` whenever we next touch the file.

- **Process notes:**
  - 5 rev iterations across one focused block with a **discussion phase up front** (~1 hour of conversation before any code). That discussion — inventorying primitives + walking through framings A/B/C with pros/cons — was the load-bearing step that the paused block 1B was missing. The "all ribbons in same direction with off-net extension" framing came out of that conversation directly; without it, we'd have iterated more revs on per-sticker direction tables and failed again.
  - Each rev was a SendMessage to a warm subagent (no fresh agent per rev), keeping context tight so the agent could relate feedback to its prior work. 5 dispatches total (1 fresh + 4 follow-ups).
  - Eyeball gate held between every rev. User feedback in sequence: "rotation centers off" (5.1), "secondary direction reversed" (5.2), "invert mask default + tune timings" (5.3), "thin boundary strokes" (5.4), "still off by a little but ship it" (close).

**Commits:** `2d42816` (rev5.0 ribbon-extension model + 12-panel layout) · `091ae3a` (rev5.1 rotation centers + auto-reset on play) · `371e4d1` (rev5.2 secondary direction fix + mask toggle + z-order) · `7d6ef22` (rev5.3 extension toggle defaults off + timing constants + pause beat) · `3dc7c25` (rev5.4 expand extension-clip 2px) · `<close>` (this commit). Branch `m9-per-card-animations` ready to merge to main.

## 2026-05-10 — Block 1B: Per-card move animations ⏸ paused — Four P0 preview revisions explored the 2D animation visual model (perpendicular cross-fade → perpendicular opposite-directions → conveyor parallel → conveyor + cross-net mask + 12-panel solved set) but did not converge on a model the user accepts. The iterate-fast cadence within one block proved too tight for the model space; paused to spin off a dedicated exploration block. Plan + preview substrate stay on branch `m9-per-card-animations` (unmerged) for the successor block to pick up; original plan's P1–P4 resume once the model is locked.
**Goal:** Each step card animates its single move once on mount / scroll-into-view, then settles on the post-move state. Click to replay. Both 2D (`FlatCubeRenderer` learns animation) AND 3D (`TwistyPlayerWrapper` play-once mode) animate on a matched-duration clock — independent triggers, same ~600 ms duration, sub-frame drift invisible. True single-RAF shared clock deferred to block 1C where cross-surface scrubbing actually requires sample-accurate sync. The start card (stepNum=0) has no move and stays static.
**Milestone:** M9.2 of the M9 plan ([roadmap entry](ROADMAP.md#milestones), [block plan](plans/m9-per-card-animations.md)). Second of three sub-blocks of M9.2 — follows block 1A (twisty everywhere, merged in `2a01a7c`) and the palette-unification drive-by; precedes block 1C (cross-surface sync moves ↔ cards ↔ player). Branch `m9-per-card-animations` off `main` HEAD `2a01a7c` (the palette-unification merge). Plan committed at `6470a62`.
**Approach:** Five phases per the plan, each = one atomic commit, app runnable throughout. (P0) static HTML preview `web/preview/flat-cube-animated.html` validates the 2D animation primitive — face-rotation + edge-cycle + cross-fade for B-face wrap stickers (R/R'/B/B'/L/U on solved + mid-scramble; light + dark theme); user eyeball gates P1. (P1) extract pure animation math into `web/src/components/cubeAnimation.ts`; extend `FlatCubeRenderer.tsx` with optional `animation?: { move; preFacelet; durationMs; nonce }` prop running a `requestAnimationFrame` loop; vitest contract per-move kinematics + animation smoke. (P2) `TwistyPlayerWrapper` adds `mode="play-once"` + `forwardRef` + `useImperativeHandle` exposing `replay()`; vitest config snapshot extension. (P3) `SolutionGrid` passes `preFacelet` (= `states[stepNum-1]`) + `move` to each step card; `SolutionCard` installs `IntersectionObserver` (first-visible → trigger), wires `onClick` for nonce-based re-trigger; both renderers fire simultaneously via the same nonce. (P4) e2e refresh — deterministic post-animation assertion (54 rects with correct `data-color` after duration + 50 ms buffer); skip mid-animation assertions; full verification sweep.
**Next:** Resume via a successor block dedicated to the 2D animation visual model — see ROADMAP backlog "M9 1B': animation visual model exploration." Branch `m9-per-card-animations` stays alive (unmerged on main) with the plan file + 4 P0 revisions intact; the successor block can either continue on this branch or fork off main and use this branch as reference. Once the visual model lands cleanly, the existing `plans/m9-per-card-animations.md` resumes at P1 in a third block.
**In progress:**
- Block opened on branch `m9-per-card-animations` off `main` HEAD `2a01a7c`. Plan committed at `6470a62` to `plans/m9-per-card-animations.md`. Approved key MVP decisions: B-face wrap = cross-fade (revisit if P0 eyeball shows it broken), shared clock = matched-duration independent triggers (true timeline coupling deferred to 1C).
- **P0 rev1 (`03d25e8`).** Perpendicular cross-fade-on-wrap. Old stickers slide outward through the active edge (clipped by face); new stickers slide inward through the same edge from the opposite direction. For wrap stickers (any move touching B-face adjacency in the net), cross-fade overlay during the slide. 6 panels (solved+R, solved+R', solved+B, solved+B', mid-scramble+L, mid-scramble+U). User eyeball: "the initial state kind of needs a little pause"; "the previous state kind of feels jumpy you can't see where it's coming from"; "I'd like the edges to slide across rather than the cubelets moving from one position." Rejected: long net translations + perpendicular crossing.
- **P0 rev2 (`493a279`).** Pre-pause (400 ms hold at pre-move) + no auto-play. Edge animation rewritten as local slides on each face: old slides OUTWARD perpendicular to the face's active edge (clipped by face); new slides INWARD from outside the same edge. ACTIVE_EDGE table per (turnedFace, adjacentFace). User eyeball: still rejected — old/new sliding in opposite directions through the same active-edge stripe read as "shrink to half width, recolor, grow back" mid-animation. Conveyor-belt model proposed.
- **P0 rev3 (`796d88f`).** Conveyor belt along edge axis: BOTH old and new strips slide in the same direction (parallel to the active edge), 3 sticker-widths over the duration. Flow direction computed empirically from FACELET_MOVES + 2D net adjacency (source-projection rule). For R move: U/F flow UP, D/B flow DOWN. Geometry verified clean at t=0.5 (continuous strip, no gap, no overlap). User eyeball: "the general idea of this slide is right but a bunch of the directions are wrong, especially the back face"; "without clipping stuff coming from outside the cube tee, it just looks goofy"; "each row/col of a face could have different directions, 3D rotation is subtler than you're giving credit for." Identified: off-net leakage + B-face direction ambiguity.
- **P0 rev4 (`3b6998c`).** Cross-net mask: single per-panel clipPath unioning all 6 face rects (the cross silhouette), applied at group level to both old and new edge-cycle strips. Per-face clipPaths dropped. Face-rotation group stays unclipped (corners pop). Panel set switched to 12 QTM moves all from solved state (R/R'/L/L'/U/U'/D/D'/F/F'/B/B'); mid-scramble panels removed per user request. Flow direction table unchanged from rev3. User eyeball: not yet given on the specific direction question; user concluded the iterate-fast cadence isn't converging and asked to pause for a focused exploration block.
- **Unresolved open question (the core of the successor block).** "Where does the new strip come from?" — empirical source-projection rule (used in rev3/rev4) maps the 3D cubie cycle to a 2D net flow direction by projecting source-face position onto the active edge axis. For R|B this gives DOWN (new arrives from above B). But user describes a different mental model: the cubies "theoretically wrap around the outside of the cube," and back-face directions need to account for 3D rotation subtleties that may not reduce to a single per-(turnedFace, adjacentFace) direction. Per user: "each row/col of a face could have different directions." Possible richer model: per-(turnedFace, adjacentFace, sticker-within-active-edge) direction, or a model that traces the 3D rotation arc through the unfolded net rather than the source-projection abstraction.

**Outcome (partial — block paused, not closed):**
- **What landed:** plan committed (`6470a62`), LOG block open (`ee28992`), 4 P0 preview revisions exploring perpendicular vs conveyor flow models and per-face vs cross-net clipping. P0 file lives at `web/preview/flat-cube-animated.html` as of rev4 (`3b6998c`) — 12-panel all-from-solved layout, cross-net mask clipping, conveyor-along-edge-axis flow. Branch `m9-per-card-animations` stays alive unmerged on main; successor block decides whether to continue on it or fork off main.
- **What didn't land:** P0 eyeball gate not passed. P1–P4 not started. The "directions are wrong on B / 3D rotation has subtleties" feedback is the unresolved core question that the successor block must answer.
- **Decisions worth remembering (for the successor):**
  - **Conveyor-along-edge-axis is the right general shape** (user: "general idea of this slide is right"). Don't go back to perpendicular cross-fades or perpendicular old/new crossings.
  - **Cross-net mask clipping works** for the off-net corners (user: "without clipping stuff coming from outside the cube tee, it just looks goofy"). The rev4 single-clipPath-per-panel approach (union of 6 face rects) is the clean implementation.
  - **12-panel all-from-solved is the right inspection set.** Mid-scramble obscures the per-move visual; one panel per QTM move from solved gives clean atomic visuals to eyeball.
  - **400 ms pre-pause + 600 ms ease-out + click-replay is the right interaction loop.** No auto-play; static initial render at pre-move; Play All + per-panel replay buttons trigger the cycle.
  - **The "where does the new strip come from?" question is the gate.** The simple source-projection rule (rev3/rev4) doesn't match user intuition for back-face cases. Possible richer models to explore: per-sticker direction within an active edge (not per-edge); arc-projection through the net rather than position-projection; explicit 3D-to-2D mapping per (turnedFace, adjacentFace, sticker) triple.
  - **Don't commit prematurely to a flow direction table.** The empirical projection rule felt right on paper but failed user eyeball for back-face cases; the successor block should resist baking in a table until visual feedback confirms it on every move's B-face panel specifically.
- **Followups:**
  - **Successor block: M9 1B': animation visual model exploration** — see ROADMAP backlog.
  - **Once the visual model is locked: resume the original plan** at `plans/m9-per-card-animations.md` P1 (FlatCubeRenderer learns animation) → P2 (TwistyPlayerWrapper play-once + replay ref) → P3 (SolutionCard wires both) → P4 (e2e refresh).
- **Process notes:**
  - The block ran for ~4 revisions of P0 before pausing. Each rev was a SendMessage to the same subagent (kept context warm); ~5–10 min wall per rev including eyeball + commit. The pattern is good for narrow fixes but couldn't handle the "explore a fundamentally different model" depth that the visual problem needed.
  - The subagent was useful for implementation but couldn't substitute for design-space exploration; that work needs the main thread + more iteration room.

**Commits:** `6470a62` (plan) · `ee28992` (open) · `03d25e8` (P0 rev1: perpendicular cross-fade) · `493a279` (P0 rev2: local perpendicular slides) · `796d88f` (P0 rev3: conveyor parallel) · `3b6998c` (P0 rev4: cross-net mask + 12-panel solved) · `<pause commit>` (this commit) — branch `m9-per-card-animations` not merged; stays alive for successor block.

## 2026-05-10 — Palette unification ✅ done — Repainted 2D net to match cubing.js stock palette so 2D and 3D cubes render identical colors; chrome palette preserved as `chromePalette` in new palettes.ts for the future picker; named-presets architecture established.
**Goal:** Make 2D and 3D cube renders ship identical colors. Since cubing.js exposes no public sticker-color override API ([issue #424](https://github.com/cubing/cubing.js/issues/424)), the only path is repainting our 2D palette to match cubing.js's stock — the chrome palette retires from the active surface but is preserved as a named preset (`chromePalette`) for the future picker. Establishes the named-presets architecture so palette swap is a one-line change once the picker UI lands.
**Milestone:** Drive-by polish on M9 ([roadmap entry](ROADMAP.md#milestones)). Sits between block 1A (twisty everywhere, just merged) and block 1B (per-card move animations).
**Approach:** Three commits. (1) New `web/src/components/palettes.ts` with `wcaPalette` (active, matching cubing.js stock) + `chromePalette` (preserved). `cubePalette.ts` re-exports `COLOR_FOR_LETTER` from `wcaPalette.colorForLetter`; `FACE_OFFSETS` + `INK_STROKE` stay (palette-agnostic geometry / stroke). (2) ROADMAP backlog additions: palette picker UI, section iv bottom-row restyle, upstream cubing.js stickerColors PR. (3) Close.
**Next:** Block closed. 2D net now ships WCA colors; chrome preserved as a named preset. Future picker UI + section iv restyle + upstream PR added to ROADMAP backlog.
**In progress:**
- Block opened on branch `palette-unification` off `main` HEAD `f99dfef` (the block 1A merge).
- **Implementation landed (`c7dfd97`).** New `web/src/components/palettes.ts` — exports `Palette` type + two named presets: `wcaPalette` (active; `U:#ffffff L:#ff8000 F:#44ee00 R:#ff0000 B:#2266ff D:#f4f400` — verified against cubing.js's `defaultPlatonicColorSchemes()` + `PGColors` enum in `web/node_modules/cubing/dist/lib/cubing/puzzle-geometry/index.js.map`) and `chromePalette` (preserved; the design-port chrome warm cream + muted accents — `U:#f5f3ee L:#e08a4a F:#5cb27a R:#d24244 B:#3a6ee0 D:#f0c64a`). `cubePalette.ts` rewritten: `COLOR_FOR_LETTER` re-exports from `wcaPalette.colorForLetter`; `FACE_OFFSETS` + `INK_STROKE` retained verbatim (palette-agnostic geometry / stroke). FlatCubeRenderer's import surface unchanged — still `import { COLOR_FOR_LETTER, FACE_OFFSETS, INK_STROKE } from "./cubePalette"`. No test references the chrome hex values directly (grep over `web/src/` + `web/e2e/` confirmed pre-edit), so vitest stayed at 53/53 with zero test edits. Bundle delta: main `index-*.js` 367.59 KB / 109.28 KB gz → 367.64 KB / 109.29 KB gz (+50 bytes raw, +0.01 KB gz — chunk hash variance from one extra import statement; effectively identical). `web/preview/flat-cube.html` was checked but its embedded `COLOR_FOR_LETTER` is a separate legacy classic-WCA palette (`#ffffff #cc1f25 #0eaf3f #ffd200 #ff7423 #1453d6`) used only inside the standalone preview's inline JS — not coupled to the production module — so it was left alone.
- **ROADMAP backlog landed (`e576242`).** Three new entries appended to `## Backlog` (after the `beam_eval_*` rename, before the `## Paused — 2x2 M5-followup` divider): "M9 backlog: palette picker UI + chromePalette preset" (with the asymmetry note — only "wca" keeps 2D + 3D matched until upstream PR lands), "M9 backlog: section iv twisty-player bottom-row restyle" (replace cubing.js's default dark-strip controls with our editorial-paper styling, callable from 1C planning), and "M9 backlog: upstream cubing.js stickerColors PR (long shot)" (focused PR exposing `TwistyPlayerConfig.faceColors`; maintainer estimate ≥1 year to land properly).
- **Final verification sweep.** `pnpm --prefix web test`: 53 passed in 4 files (148ms) ✓. `pnpm --prefix web lint`: clean ✓. `pnpm --prefix web build`: clean (100ms; bundle shape per implementation note above) ✓. `pnpm --prefix web test:e2e` (Playwright stub): 14 passed + 2 skipped (4.4s) ✓. `PLAYWRIGHT_REAL_BACKEND=1 pnpm --prefix web test:e2e`: 16 passed (5.5s) ✓ — including the 3D-toggle + split-toggle specs that exercise the now-WCA-colored flat side. `uv run pytest`: 707 passed, 1 deselected (36.56s) ✓ (no Python touched). `uv run ruff check src/ tests/ scripts/codegen/`: clean ✓.

**Outcome:**
- **Block goal met.** 2D net cards now render with cubing.js stock colors (`#ffffff / #ff8000 / #44ee00 / #ff0000 / #2266ff / #f4f400`); 3D twisty-player ships the same palette by default. Mode toggle 2D ↔ 3D no longer shows a color shift — the visual divergence accepted at the close of block 1A is closed at the surface level. Chrome palette preserved verbatim in `chromePalette` for the future picker; no design data lost.
- **Decisions worth recording:**
  - **WCA over chrome was forced, not chosen.** Cubing.js's [issue #424](https://github.com/cubing/cubing.js/issues/424) confirms there's no public sticker-color override API, and the maintainer estimate to surface `TwistyPlayerConfig.faceColors` is ≥1 year. Symmetry between 2D and 3D therefore requires *we* match *them*, not vice versa — chrome cannot reach the 3D path until upstream lands. Keeping the chrome palette as a preserved preset (rather than deleting it) is the cheap right thing: zero cost today, full restore-path the moment the picker / upstream PR exists.
  - **`FlatCubeRenderer` import surface unchanged.** `COLOR_FOR_LETTER`, `FACE_OFFSETS`, `INK_STROKE` are still the three names exported from `cubePalette.ts` — the change is internal (sourced from a named preset module) rather than at the consumer boundary. This kept the implementation diff tight and made the tests + e2e specs trivially pass-through. The future picker becomes a one-line swap of the `COLOR_FOR_LETTER` re-export plus a thin React context provider.
  - **`FACE_OFFSETS` + `INK_STROKE` stay in `cubePalette.ts`.** They're not palette data — `FACE_OFFSETS` is geometry (URFDLB facelet offsets) and `INK_STROKE` is the dark sticker outline that reads well over any plausible preset. Pulling them into `palettes.ts` would have coupled per-palette swap to layout constants that don't change. Single source of truth: layout constants live with the renderer, palette data lives in `palettes.ts`.
  - **`web/preview/flat-cube.html` left untouched.** Its inlined `COLOR_FOR_LETTER` is a legacy classic-WCA approximation embedded for standalone-preview portability (the preview opens via `open <file>` with no Vite resolve). It's not coupled to the production module and would re-introduce a build-time dependency to wire it in. Filed implicitly as a future cleanup if/when we re-spin previews against the live source tree.
- **Followups (in ROADMAP backlog):**
  - Palette picker UI + chromePalette as a swappable preset (with the asymmetry caveat — only "wca" keeps 2D + 3D matched until upstream PR lands).
  - Section iv twisty-player bottom-row restyle (replace cubing.js's dark-strip controls with editorial-paper styled play/pause/scrubber driven by the imperative API; coordinate with 1C cross-surface sync planning).
  - Upstream cubing.js `stickerColors` / `TwistyPlayerConfig.faceColors` PR (long shot; ≥1 year to land properly per maintainer).
- **Process:** Small block (1 LOG-open + 1 implementation + 1 ROADMAP + 1 LOG-close = 4 commits), inline (no subagent), under 30 min wall. No deviations from brief; no test edits required (no test asserted chrome hex values pre-change).

**Commits:** `8174cf7` (open) · `c7dfd97` (implementation) · `e576242` (backlog) · close (this commit). No separate plan file — the brief itself was the plan.

## 2026-05-10 — Block 1A: Twisty everywhere (replace iso) ✅ done — Replaced homegrown IsoCubeRenderer with cubing.js `<twisty-player>` in all 3D contexts (card iso mode, dual split right pane); added section iv "watch the solve" animated full-solve player; iso fully retired (-698 lines). Palette divergence between 2D chrome and 3D cubing.js stock accepted as a design trade-off per upstream issue #424 (no public sticker-color API).
**Goal:** Replace the homegrown `IsoCubeRenderer` with cubing.js's `<twisty-player>` in **all** 3D contexts: cards in `iso` mode, the right pane of `dual` (split) mode, AND a new **section iv "watch the solve"** animated full-solve player below the card grid. Iso retires entirely. `cubing@0.63.3` is already in `package.json` (no new dep). cubing.js has no public palette override API ([issue #424](https://github.com/cubing/cubing.js/issues/424)) — accept palette divergence: 2D net keeps the chrome palette; all 3D contexts ship cubing.js stock colors. **No per-card animation yet** (cards render at the post-move static state via `experimentalSetupAlg` + `experimentalSetupAnchor="end"`); **no cross-surface sync yet** (sections operate independently). Block 1A establishes the 3D infrastructure end-to-end.
**Milestone:** M9.2 of the M9 plan ([roadmap entry](ROADMAP.md#milestones), [M9 plan](plans/m9-ui.md#m92--3d-rendering-via-twisty-player)). First of three sub-blocks expanding the original M9.2 scope into a synchronized animation centerpiece — see `Followups` for 1B (per-card move animations) and 1C (cross-surface sync). Branch `m9-twisty-player` off main HEAD `e0a063b` (the bin/site dev-runner drive-by; no LOG entry of its own). Plan committed at `fd8fddf` (`plans/m9-twisty-everywhere.md`).
**Approach:** Six phases per the plan: (P0) verify cubing/twisty resolves under Vite + add JSX type augmentation `web/src/types/twisty-player.d.ts` for React 19 + scaffold `TwistyPlayerWrapper.tsx` (imperative, ref-based, `new TwistyPlayer(config)` inside `useEffect`) + static HTML preview at `web/preview/twisty-player.html` covering 90/130/220 px sizes × static + animated × `experimental-setup-alg` round-trip; (P1) swap iso → twisty-static in `SolutionCard.tsx` `iso` branch + tune size/camera; (P2) swap iso → twisty-static in `dual` branch (`.render-pair` row stays); (P3) add `<SectionFour>` with animated `<TwistyPlayer mode="animated">` rendering `scramble` setup-alg + `solution` alg, plus title hairline; (P4) retire `IsoCubeRenderer.tsx` + `iso-cube.html` preview + iso vitest + iso-related e2e selectors; (P5) e2e refresh covering twisty-static cards (Playwright shadow-DOM `>>>` selectors per cubing.js custom-element shape) + section iv presence + scramble→solve→player smoke. Each phase = atomic commit, app runnable throughout. Lazy-load `TwistyPlayerWrapper` via `React.lazy` so first-paint is unaffected on routes that don't need 3D.
**Next:** Block closed. All 3D paths route through `<twisty-player>`; iso retired with zero grep hits in `web/src/`/`web/e2e/`/`web/preview/`. Block 1B (per-card move animations on a synced 2D + 3D clock) opens next. Branch `m9-twisty-player` ready to merge.

**Outcome:**
- **Block goal met.** All three render modes (net / iso (now twisty-static) / split (flat + twisty-static)) work end-to-end. Section iv "watch the solve" appears post-solve with cubing.js's bottom-row play/pause/scrubber driving an animated 360 px twisty-player. Iso fully retired: 3 files deleted (`IsoCubeRenderer.tsx`, `IsoCubeRenderer.test.ts`, `iso-cube.html`); `cubePalette.ts` colocated with FlatCubeRenderer; zero `IsoCubeRenderer` grep hits across `web/src/` + `web/e2e/` + `web/preview/`. User flow: scramble + solve → cards render in either chrome 2D or cubing.js-stock 3D or split (one of each per card); section iv plays the full sequence on demand.
- **Decisions worth recording:**
  - **Palette divergence accepted as the locked design trade-off.** `cubing.js` exposes no public sticker-color override API ([issue #424](https://github.com/cubing/cubing.js/issues/424) confirms; maintainer estimate ≥1 year). The chrome palette unification the iso block achieved is now broken — 2D net stays chrome (`#f5f3ee` warm cream, designer-tuned greens), 3D ships cubing.js stock (true white, saturated WCA). Mode toggle 2D ↔ 3D shows a visible color shift. This is a *design* trade-off, not a bug — documented in the LOG and in the plan's "Key decision baked in" section. The fix path if we ever pursue it: contribute the `stickerColors` PR upstream (the internal plumbing in `PuzzleGeometry.get3d({stickerColors})` exists; it's just not surfaced to `TwistyPlayerConfig`).
  - **Imperative React wrapper, not JSX.** `TwistyPlayer` is a Lit-style custom element; the recommended path per upstream is `new TwistyPlayer(config); ref.appendChild(player)` inside `useEffect`. The JSX runtime augmentation in `web/src/types/twisty-player.d.ts` exists for ad-hoc usage, but production paths use the imperative wrapper — gives clean unmount, lets us pass non-attribute config (e.g. `experimentalMovePressCancelOptions` if/when needed), and keeps test surface small.
  - **`buildTwistyPlayerConfig` extracted to a sibling `.ts`.** ESLint's `react-refresh/only-export-components` forbids non-component exports from `.tsx`. The clean separation also means the vitest contract tests the config helper directly — no jsdom, no `vi.mock`, no `@testing-library/react`. The contract surface ("wrapper passes the right `TwistyPlayerConfig` shape") is testable as a pure function.
  - **`cubePalette.ts` colocated with `FlatCubeRenderer`.** Was in `web/src/styles/` when both Flat and Iso shared it; with iso retired, single-consumer modules colocate with their consumer. If a third consumer ever shows up, hoist back to `styles/`.
  - **`React.lazy` deferred.** Plan flagged it as a possible bundle-size win. Build report shows main shell +44 KB gz from the cubing.js entry; three.js + the 3D scene + puzzle-geometry are already auto-split by cubing.js into lazy chunks. The 44 KB main delta isn't worth the consumption complexity (`<Suspense>` boundary, fallback rendering) for users who land on the page and immediately interact with 3D — which is the entire point of the demo. Revisit only if 1B's animation work or telemetry shows the main-shell delta hurts first paint.
  - **`RenderMode = "net" | "iso" | "dual"` rename deferred.** The value `"iso"` is a misnomer post-block. Rename cascades through App state, RenderModeSwitch entries, and e2e selectors — held until 1B/1C surface other API tweaks worth bundling.
  - **`tempo-scale=0` flicker (research pitfall #2) doesn't manifest** at `cubing@0.63.3` with `experimental-setup-anchor="end"`. Eyeballed the static preview before P1; clean settle on first paint. No `opacity:0` + rAF dance needed in the wrapper.
  - **`sol-card-0` is the canonical e2e target** for renderer-mode tests under stub mode. Stub net returns `moves=[]` so step cards don't render — `sol-card-1+` only exist under real backend. Same convention as the iso block.
  - **Section iv state-agnostic API.** SectionFour returns `null` on `solutionAlg.trim() === ""`. Empty-string at the prop boundary handles initial load, post-clear, post-state-paste (paste with no solve), and the edge case of `solver returning []` (already-solved input). Caller doesn't need to track "is solution null vs empty"; the component does the right thing either way.
  - **E2E asserts wrapper presence, not shadow-DOM pixels.** Twisty-player's shadow root + WebGL canvas are version-coupled internal shape; `[data-testid="twisty-cube"]` on the wrapper `<div>` is the contract surface we control. Keeps the e2e suite stable across cubing.js minor-version bumps.
- **Followups (recorded for later blocks, not done here):**
  - **Block 1B — per-card move animations** (already noted below). Each card animates its single move once on mount/scroll-into-view in BOTH 2D and 3D on a shared clock. The 2D side is the speculative work — face rotation + wrap-sticker handling across the unfolded net's B-face teleport. Static HTML preview pattern applies hard.
  - **Block 1C — cross-surface sync.** Sections ii/iii/iv share `activeStepIdx`; click any surface, scrub the others. MovesGrid cells become interactive.
  - **Type rename** `RenderMode = "net" | "iso" | "dual"` → `"net" | "3d" | "dual"`. Documented in P4. Bundle with whatever other API tweaks 1B/1C produce.
  - **`React.lazy` revisit** if first-paint metrics regress or the main-shell delta becomes load-bearing. Mechanical change once decided.
  - **Upstream cubing.js PR** for `TwistyPlayerConfig.faceColors` would close the palette gap. Out of M9 scope; flagged as a long-term option if we want chrome palette back across all surfaces.
- **Process notes:**
  - One subagent per phase (P0–P5, six total) + main thread for plan/log/eyeball orchestration. Each subagent committed its phase atomically; main verified scope via `git log --oneline` between dispatches. The user's "no flicker on the static cubes" eyeball gated P0 → P1 (~30 sec investment, removed the opacity-dance contingency from the wrapper).
  - The cubePalette colocation in P4 (rather than as a separate post-block refactor) shows the value of bundling related cleanup into the block that creates the orphan. Single commit, single message, full context.
  - Plan-mode plan staged at `/Users/griff/.claude/plans/compressed-hopping-treasure.md`; same content shipped to `plans/m9-twisty-everywhere.md` for repo-tracked reference. Mirrors prior blocks' "plan SHA → open-block SHA → phase SHAs" pattern.

 — Wired the design-port v2 render-mode stubs: ported svgIso to a chrome-palette IsoCubeRenderer, enabled the 3D and split (flat + iso side-by-side) buttons, halved per-card sizes for split mode
**Goal:** Wire the v2 stubs left behind by `design-port-sequence`: enable the **iso** (3D) and **dual** (split) modes in `RenderModeSwitch`. Port the handoff bundle's `svgIso` to a chrome-palette `IsoCubeRenderer` that matches `FlatCubeRenderer`'s identity (same color map, ink stroke, strokeMul). Net-only behavior unchanged; this is purely additive at the section iii render layer.
**Milestone:** drive-by completion of the design-port v2 stubs on M9.1 ([roadmap entry](ROADMAP.md#milestones), [M9 plan](plans/m9-ui.md)). Plan landed at `plans/3d-iso-and-split.md` (`13827ba`).
**Approach:** Branch `3d-iso-and-split` off `main` HEAD `1531692` (the design-port merge commit). `svgIso` math comes from `/tmp/rubik-handoff/design_handoff_rubik_solver/source/cube.js:81-154` — ~73 lines of pure SVG (30° iso projection, U+F+R faces with shading, gap insets, viewBox auto-fit). Three phases: (1) `IsoCubeRenderer.tsx` + `web/preview/iso-cube.html` static preview + vitest contract — component is import-ready but not yet wired; (2) thread `renderMode` through `SolutionGrid` → `SolutionCard`, enable the 3D button, e2e flips assertions from disabled to active; (3) split mode renders both renderers side-by-side with halved per-card sizes (`DUAL_SIZE_BY_COLS`). Each phase = one atomic commit, app runnable throughout. App.tsx already has `renderMode` state and threads it to SolutionGrid — only SolutionCard plumbing + the new component are missing.
**Next:** Block closed. All three render modes (net / iso / split) work end-to-end against both stub-net and real LN model. Branch `3d-iso-and-split` ready to merge. Remaining design-port follow-on items still open: 1-column row layout with prose descriptions; wire 2×2 header toggle through to backend model selection.

Render order U → R → F (F drawn last so it overlaps in iso projection). Auto-fit viewBox from the 8 projected cube corners + `sw * 4` pad. `data-face` and `data-color` attrs baked into every polygon for downstream e2e introspection. Chrome palette + ink stroke imported from new `web/src/styles/cubePalette.ts` (factored out of FlatCubeRenderer in the same commit — single source of truth, both renderers consume it). Static HTML preview at `web/preview/iso-cube.html` shows 3 states (solved + length-7 scramble + length-20 scramble) × 3 sizes (90 / 130 / 220 px) × light + dark themes; pure inline JS duplicating the iso math, no build step. SolutionGrid destructures and forwards `renderMode` to every SolutionCard (start + mapped step cards). New e2e test (`solution-grid.spec.ts`): scrolls to `sol-card-0`, asserts 54 `<rect[data-pos]>` (Flat), clicks 3D, asserts 0 rects + 27 `<polygon[data-face]>` (iso), clicks 2D, asserts 54 rects return. **Deviation noted:** original brief targeted `sol-card-1`; subagent switched to `sol-card-0` because `RUBIK_USE_STUB_NET=1` returns `moves=[]` from `/api/solve` — no step cards exist in stub mode, but the renderer code path is identical, so `sol-card-0` exercises it just as well without any scramble/solve setup.
- **Phase 3 landed (`3f0c4bf`).** SolutionCard's `dual` branch wraps both renderers in `.render-pair` flex row with distinct testids `flat-cube-pair` / `iso-cube-pair` (so dual-mode e2e can target both unambiguously without colliding with the default `flat-cube` / `iso-cube` solo-mode testids). SolutionGrid adds `DUAL_SIZE_BY_COLS = { 1: 200, 2: 160, 3: 100, 4: 90, 6: 60 }`; `sizePx` picked off renderMode (dual gets the halved table, net + iso get the original `CUBE_SIZE_BY_COLS`). RenderModeSwitch's `dual` entry flipped to `disabled: false`. New `.render-pair` CSS — flex row, `align-items: center` (iso renders taller than flat — center vertically), 12px gap; `.cols-6 .render-pair { gap: 8px }` override since the cubes themselves stay legible at 60px and the negative space was the cramped axis. New e2e: clicks split, asserts both `flat-cube-pair` + `iso-cube-pair` testids appear inside `sol-card-0`, asserts `<rect[data-pos]>` count = 54 AND `<polygon[data-face]>` count = 27, clicks 2D, asserts both -pair testids gone.
- **Final verification sweep.** `uv run pytest`: 707 passed, 1 deselected, 35.66s. `pnpm --prefix web test` (vitest): 59 passed, 144ms. `pnpm --prefix web test:e2e` (Playwright stub): 14 passed + 1 skipped (real-backend), 3.8s. `PLAYWRIGHT_REAL_BACKEND=1 pnpm --prefix web test:e2e`: 15 passed, 4.3s — including the 3 stub-also specs run again under real backend plus the previously-skipped real-backend `scramble-solve` spec. `pnpm --prefix web build`: clean, 51ms. `pnpm --prefix web lint`: clean. `uv run ruff check src/ tests/ scripts/codegen/`: clean (none touched).

**Outcome:**
- **Block goal met.** All three render modes work end-to-end against both stub and real backends. User flow: scramble + solve → click net → cards show flat-only; click 3D → iso-only with chrome palette + 30° iso projection; click split → both side-by-side with halved sizes. RenderModeSwitch no longer carries any disabled v2 stubs.
- **Decisions worth recording:**
  - **Factored `COLOR_FOR_LETTER` + `FACE_OFFSETS` + `INK_STROKE` into `web/src/styles/cubePalette.ts`** rather than duplicating the chrome palette across FlatCubeRenderer and IsoCubeRenderer. Single source of truth — when the design changes (or 2x2 lands and shares the palette), one edit. Did this in Phase 1's commit alongside the iso renderer rather than as a separate refactor commit.
  - **27 polygons in iso, not 54.** Iso views show only the front-top-right of the cube (U + F + R). Hidden D / L / B faces are never drawn — visual contract, not a bug. The renderer's existence is "what the human sees of the cube"; full state lives in net mode and in the underlying facelet string. Documented in the IsoCubeRenderer test file via the polygon-count assertion.
  - **Distinct `-pair` testids in dual mode rather than reusing `flat-cube` / `iso-cube`.** Keeps testid uniqueness — solo-mode tests can't accidentally match the dual mode's renderers, and dual-mode tests don't have to count or filter. Cost: tests that want to introspect either renderer in dual mode need to know the `-pair` suffix. Cheap.
  - **`DUAL_SIZE_BY_COLS` halves the per-card render size, not the per-renderer size.** Each renderer in the pair receives the table value directly; the table values already account for "two renderers per card" so SolutionCard doesn't halve again. Considered the alternative (each renderer gets `CUBE_SIZE_BY_COLS / 2`), but the explicit lookup table is easier to tune by column.
  - **`sol-card-0` (start card) is the canonical e2e target for renderer-mode tests under stub mode.** Stub net returns `moves=[]` so step cards don't render — `sol-card-1` doesn't exist. Phase 2's subagent caught this and adapted; Phase 3 followed the same pattern. The renderer code path is identical between start and step cards, so this is a sound substitution. Real-backend specs that need step-card content already do their own scramble + solve setup.
  - **Brief said F-tinted = `#58aa75`; actual = `#58ab75`.** `Math.round(178 * 0.96) = 171 = 0xab`, not 170 = 0xaa. Phase 1's test asserts the actual computed hex, not the brief-stated value. Caught at write time, not later.
  - **Tightened `.render-pair` gap from 12 → 8 at cols=6** rather than shrinking the 60 px renderer size. The cubes stay legible at 60 px; the negative space was the cramped axis. Single override on `.cols-6 .render-pair`.
  - **Stroke-aliasing risk flagged in plan turned out moot.** `strokeMul = 0.025` in unit space (viewBox spans ~5 units) reads consistently across all sizes since the iso projection uses unit-space strokes, not pixel-space. No size-dependent override needed at any of 60 / 90 / 130 / 220 px.
- **Followups (not done in this block):**
  - **1-column row layout with prose descriptions.** Still a v2 strand on `ColumnsSwitch`. Different layout signal entirely (rows + prose, not columns of cards) — separate block worth scoping when needed.
  - **2×2 cube logic toggle** — header switch is still local UI state only. Backend already has 2×2; wiring touches `apiHealth` model selection + env reload. Separate block.
  - **Iso-only e2e introspection helpers.** `data-face` is on every polygon but not exercised yet by any spec. If future tests want per-face validation under iso mode, the attrs are baked in.
- **Process notes:**
  - One subagent per phase (3 total) + main thread for plan/log/merge orchestration. Each subagent committed its phase atomically; main verified scope via `git diff <prev> <new> --stat` between dispatches before kicking off the next.
  - The cubePalette refactor inside Phase 1 (recommended in the brief, executed by subagent) shows the value of "DRY when adding the second consumer" — both renderers stayed import-ready throughout subsequent phases without coupling.

**Commits:** `13827ba` (plan) · `36356c7` (open block) · `01a976f` (P1 iso renderer + static preview + cubePalette refactor) · `313e50d` (P2 wire 3D toggle) · `3f0c4bf` (P3 split mode) · `<this commit>` (close)

## 2026-05-09 — Design port: Sequence layout ✅ done — Editorial rewrite of the M9.1 web demo: Fraunces + JetBrains Mono on warm cream paper with rust accent, three roman-numeraled sections (i. starting state / ii. moves to apply / iii. solution), card-grid solution view with column + render toggles
**Goal:** Port the Claude Designer "Sequence" handoff into the React/Vite web app — full visual chrome (920px column on warm cream paper, Fraunces + JetBrains Mono, rust accent, three roman-numeraled sections `i. starting state` / `ii. moves to apply` / `iii. solution`, italic *Solved.* footer with model meta) on top of the existing 2D net renderer. v1 ships net-only; 3D iso, split view, 1-column row variant, and 2×2-toggle behavior are stubbed.
**Milestone:** drive-by visual rewrite on M9.1 ([roadmap entry](ROADMAP.md#milestones), [M9 plan](plans/m9-ui.md)). Effectively replaces the strip-view UX from `solver-demo-polish` with the editorial "Sequence" layout while keeping that branch's parser/validator/cube-rendering plumbing.
**Approach:** Branch `design-port-sequence` off `main` HEAD `6c5baa0` (the merge commit that brought `solver-demo-polish` into main). Reference HTML copied to `web/preview/sequence-reference.html` — the pixel target. Six phases per `plans/design-port-sequence.md`: (0) foundations — design tokens + Google Fonts + base reset; (1) layout shell + header + *Solved.* footer; (2) section i — six-face state grid with auto-advance + cascading paste, length-pack chip; (3) section ii — ruled letter grid; (4) section iii — solution cards with column + render toggles, replacing `MoveStripView`/`StepControls`/`MoveList`; (5) cleanup + e2e refresh. Each phase = one atomic commit, app stays runnable throughout. Salvage: `parseMoves`, `validateFacelet`, `FlatCubeRenderer` core, `applyMove`, `faceletMoves`, API client. Delete: `MoveStripView`, `StateField`, `MovesField`, `ScrambleControls`, `StepControls`.
**Next:** Visual review against `web/preview/sequence-reference.html` — `pnpm dev` already running on :5173 in this session. If pixel-fidelity holds, merge `design-port-sequence` → `main` with `--no-ff` and delete the branch. Follow-on backlog items if you want to extend: 3D iso renderer + split view (the `cube.js` helpers in `/tmp/rubik-handoff/.../cube.js` are portable verbatim); 1-column row layout with prose descriptions; wire 2×2 header toggle through to backend model selection; revisit dropped paste-moves UX if external solver paste-back lands as a real workflow.
**In progress:**
- Block opened. `solver-demo-polish` (14 commits, block officially closed at `5d361a1`) merged into main with `--no-ff` (merge commit `6c5baa0`), source branch deleted. Branched `design-port-sequence` from there. Reference HTML copied to `web/preview/sequence-reference.html` (332KB single-file standalone — opens offline, no deps).
- **Plan landed at `plans/design-port-sequence.md`.** Six phases (0–5) plus close. Locked decisions documented: v1 scope = 2D net only with full chrome (user-confirmed); paste-moves UX dropped (no analog in reference; state-paste survives in section i); 2×2 toggle is local UI state only.
- **Phase 0 landed (`c0569a0`).** `web/src/styles/tokens.css` with the 6 design tokens (`--bg #f3efe6`, `--paper #fbf8f1`, `--ink #131210`, `--dim #6f6a60`, `--rule #d9d2c2`, `--accent #c64a23`). Google Fonts links in `web/index.html` (Fraunces opsz 9..144 weights 300/400/500, JetBrains Mono 400/500/600) with preconnect. Body reset in `index.css` consumes the tokens. `.serif` class exposes Fraunces. No component changes — existing UI just colour-shifts onto warm paper.
- **Phase 1 landed (`6fa874f`).** Wrap App in 920px `.col` container. Wordmark component (`<h1 class="serif wordmark">rubik <em>solver</em></h1>` — italic accent on "solver", Fraunces 32px opsz 144, white-space nowrap). CubeSizeSwitch in header right (2×2 / 3×3 segmented control via `.col-seg-inline`; local UI state only, no backend wiring in v1). SolvedFooter with italic *Solved.* + dim prose + uppercase meta tag, fed from `SolveStats.time_ms` + `Health.model_path` via `formatMeta()`. Class names mirror the reference for direct side-by-side compare. Existing strip + animation player still rendered between header and footer (transitional).
- **Phase 2 landed (`c5a2ff9`).** New `SectionHeader`, `StateGrid`, `LengthPack` components. Section i header carries `clear` link-btn + hairline + length-pack chip on the right. Six-face state grid (display order U L F R B D, but each input carries its URFDLB storage offset — paste of 54 chars at any input distributes by storage position so external-solver paste round-trips). Auto-advance on the 9th char, backspace-jump-back, partial-paste cascade in display order. Drafts are local; commit only when assembled string validates. Render-time sync (React docs pattern, not `useEffect`) re-derives drafts on external Scramble / Clear without losing typing focus. Native `<input type="range">` styled with `::-webkit-slider-thumb` / `::-moz-range-thumb` to match the reference's 11px paper knob with 1.5px accent border on a 1px rule track. Deletes: `StateField.tsx`, `ScrambleControls.tsx`. Solve button stays in a transitional location until Phase 4.
- **Phase 3 landed (`41a9424`).** New `MovesGrid` component: 10-column ruled grid, one cell per move, primes rendered as `′` in italic accent. Empty trailing cells dashed at 55% opacity with `·` glyph. When >10 moves, rows carry the italic-accent roman tag (`i.` `ii.` `iii.` `iv.`) on their right edge. Wired `scrambleMoves` state from `apiScramble.moves`; cleared on Clear / state-paste / mid-solve. SectionHeader's right shows "N moves" / "1 move". Always renders ≥1 row so section ii holds rhythm even when state was pasted (no scramble → "0 moves" + dashed dots). Deletes: `MovesField.tsx` (textarea-based moves paste UX has no analog in the reference; revisit if missed), `MoveList.tsx` (the ordered solution list — section iii's cards in Phase 4 carry this information inline).
- **Phase 4 landed (`501e382`).** New `SolutionGrid`, `SolutionCard`, `RenderModeSwitch`, `ColumnsSwitch`. Section iii header right slot carries Solve button (`.btn-pill.solid`) + hairline + render-mode switch (2D / 3D / split — 3D and split disabled in v1 with title-attr v2 reasons) + columns switch (1 / 2 / 3 / 4 / 6 — 1 disabled in v1, 3 default). CSS `--cols` drives grid template. Per-card layout: top row = `step-num` (mono dim, zero-padded `00`) left + `move-glyph` (Fraunces, italic-accent modifier) right. Below: cube preview at column-dependent size (220 / 200 / 130 / 130 / 90 px). Step-00 card is the starting state — dashed border + italic dim "start" label. Active card = accent border + 1px box-shadow ring; click selects, replacing the old stepper. `FlatCubeRenderer` restyled: chrome palette (`#f5f3ee` / `#d24244` / `#5cb27a` / `#f0c64a` / `#e08a4a` / `#3a6ee0`, designer-tuned), ink stroke (`#131210`) at 0.025 sticker-multiplier (thinner than the prior 0.04), 1px corner radius. Drops the V2 in-SVG label `Overlay[]` — labels now live in card chrome. States memoized per (`scrambleState`, `solution`) so changing `activeIdx` only re-renders the highlight. SolveButton restyled to `.btn-pill.solid` (ink fill, paper text, accent on hover). Deletes: `MoveStripView.tsx`, `StepControls.tsx`, plus the entire transitional animation-player section in `App.tsx`.
- **Phase 5 landed (`e615b6c`).** E2E spec refresh. Renames: `state-moves-paste.spec.ts` → `state-grid-paste.spec.ts` (paste-into-U cascades to all 6 face inputs; auto-advance U→L on 9th char; backspace-jump-back L→U). `strip-view.spec.ts` → `solution-grid.spec.ts` (columns toggle drives `--cols`; v2 toggles disabled; card click sets `data-active`; cube-size header toggle moves active state). `scramble-solve.spec.ts` updated to target `sol-card-0` SVG + `moves-grid` cell count + `solved-footer` (drops Surprise-Me sub-test — no surprise button in `LengthPack`). `renderer.spec.ts` targets the SVG inside `sol-card-0`. `health.spec.ts` rewritten — drops the brittle `health-json` text dump (the new design intentionally omits it) and asserts the load-bearing boundary: `scramble-button` enables once `/api/health` resolves with `warmup_done=true`. Deletes: `step-through.spec.ts` (auto-play stepper no longer exists; card-click navigation covered by `solution-grid.spec.ts`), `web/preview/strip-view.html` (orphaned). 12 / 13 e2e green (1 real-backend skipped).
**Outcome:**
- All six planned phases shipped as one-commit-each on `design-port-sequence`. Reviewable diff per phase; app runnable at every checkpoint.
- New visual surface: 920px centered column, warm cream paper, Fraunces + JetBrains Mono, rust accent. Three roman-numeraled sections (i. starting state / ii. moves to apply / iii. solution), italic *Solved.* footer with model+time meta tag.
- New components shipped: `Wordmark`, `CubeSizeSwitch`, `SectionHeader`, `StateGrid` (auto-advance + cascading paste), `LengthPack` (styled native range), `MovesGrid` (ruled letter grid), `SolutionGrid` + `SolutionCard` (column-driven sizing), `RenderModeSwitch` + `ColumnsSwitch` (with v2 stubs), `SolvedFooter`. `FlatCubeRenderer` restyled to chrome palette + ink stroke + 1px rx; lost its `Overlay[]` prop. `SolveButton` restyled to `.btn-pill.solid`.
- Deletes: `StateField`, `ScrambleControls`, `MovesField`, `MoveList`, `MoveStripView`, `StepControls`, `web/preview/strip-view.html`, `web/e2e/strip-view.spec.ts`, `web/e2e/step-through.spec.ts`, `web/e2e/state-moves-paste.spec.ts`.
- Tests: 42 vitest unit tests pass (parseMoves / validateFacelet / applyMove unchanged from the salvage). 12 / 13 Playwright e2e tests pass (1 skipped is real-backend, gated on `PLAYWRIGHT_REAL_BACKEND=1`). `pnpm build` + `pnpm lint` clean.
- v2 stubs documented inline in `RenderModeSwitch` / `ColumnsSwitch` (disabled buttons, title-attr reasons). The `cube.js` from the handoff bundle (`/tmp/rubik-handoff/.../source/cube.js`) ships ready-to-port `svgIso` + `rotY2` for the v2 round.
- Decision worth remembering: state-grid paste behavior diverges intentionally from the reference. The reference cascades by VISUAL position (U L F R B D); a paste of 54 chars at U would put chars 9-17 into L, breaking URFDLB. Our impl distributes by STORAGE offset on a 54-char paste, falls back to display-order cascade for partial pastes. External solvers emit URFDLB; honoring that ordering means a paste at any input round-trips correctly. Documented in `StateGrid.tsx`.
- Decision worth remembering: dropped the textarea moves-paste UX (`MovesField`) because the reference treats section ii as display-only. Section i's state-paste path remains the supported way to bring an external state in. Revisit if external-solver workflows ask for moves-paste-back.
- Decision worth remembering: chose React's render-time sync pattern (the official "storing information from previous renders" pattern) over `useEffect` for `StateGrid`'s prop-to-draft sync. Same lint rule (`react-hooks/set-state-in-effect`) that previously bit `StepControls.tsx`. Pattern: `if (prop !== prevProp) { setPrevProp(prop); setLocalState(derive(prop)); }` — no useEffect needed, focus survives.
**Commits:** `8e99d53` (plan) · `de7e66d` (open block) · `c0569a0` (P0 tokens+fonts) · `6fa874f` (P1 shell+chrome) · `c5a2ff9` (P2 state grid) · `41a9424` (P3 moves grid) · `501e382` (P4 solution cards) · `e615b6c` (P5 e2e refresh)

## 2026-05-09 — Solver demo polish ✅ done — Web demo now an inspection tool: length picker (1–30 + Surprise Me), state/moves paste/copy with validation, strip view as primary visualization with V2 in-SVG labels and Small/Medium/Large cube sizing
**Goal:** Make the M9.1 web demo more useful for inspection, not just demonstration. Scope: (1) replace the hardcoded length-14 button with a length picker (range 1–30, plus a "Surprise Me" entry); (2) add cube-state and move-sequence text fields at the top — both copy-out (to share) and paste-in (to reproduce / inspect a particular state); (3) make the per-move strip view the primary visualization — show every intermediate state stacked vertically (or in a compact grid) with the move label that produced it, so a human can validate moves frame-by-frame; demote the existing animation player to secondary. Pulls forward most of M9.3's "alt input + polish" plus a new visualization mode that wasn't in any prior plan.
**Milestone:** drive-by polish on M9.1; effectively pulls M9.3 scope forward + adds a new strip-view UX ([roadmap entry](ROADMAP.md#milestones), [M9 plan](plans/m9-ui.md))
**Approach:** Branch `solver-demo-polish` from main HEAD `361d29a` (the M9.1 merge commit). Detailed plan lands in `plans/solver-demo-polish.md` via plan mode before any implementation commits — open questions to resolve there: paste-notation parsing (cubing.js's `Alg`/`KPattern` API vs hand-roll over the existing `applyMove` table), strip-view layout (vertical scroll vs grid wrap; row sizing at length=14 vs length=30), length-picker UX (slider + numeric vs dropdown + "Surprise Me"), and whether the move-sequence paste path also implies an "Apply moves to current state" button distinct from solve.
**Next:** Block closed. Demo working end-to-end with the new inspector controls. M9.2 (3D rendering via cubing.js `<twisty-player>`) and the residual M9.3 polish (mobile layout, share-URL, manual color-grid entry, animation speed slider) carry forward per `plans/m9-ui.md`. Open a new block when next M9 phase begins.
**In progress:**
- Block opened. Branch `solver-demo-polish` from main HEAD `361d29a`. M9.1 (`m9.1-solver-demo`, 11 commits) merged via `--no-ff` and the source branch deleted.
- **Plan landed at `plans/solver-demo-polish.md`** (commit `803e43e`). Six atomic commits planned: (1) plan, (2) `parseMoves.ts` parser+formatter, (3) `ScrambleControls.tsx` length picker, (4) `StateField`/`MovesField` paste/copy, (5) `MoveStripView` + animation-player demote, (6) close. Locked decisions: hand-roll parser over MoveStr (QTM-only), 6-col grid strip layout, slider+Surprise length picker, paste-moves replaces solution only.
- **Commit 2 landed (`9ae2bd2`).** `web/src/state/parseMoves.ts` + test. `parseMoves(s) → MoveStr[]` validates each whitespace-split token against a `Set<string>` built from `Object.keys(FACELET_MOVES)` (single source of truth — parser can't drift from the move table the network was trained on); throws `ParseMovesError` with `{ token, index }` (1-based) on the first invalid token. `formatMoves(moves) → string` joins with single spaces; `parseMoves(formatMoves(xs))` round-trips. 27 vitest cases: valid sequences (single, prime, sexy, length-14 explicit, length-30 programmatic, empty, whitespace-only), whitespace tolerance (leading/trailing, collapsed multi-space, tabs+newlines), round-trip across [], single, typical, all-12, and rejection of `R2`/`Rw`/`M`/`x`/lowercase `r`/`RR`/detached `'U` as token-2/`R2` mid-sequence/`garbage` — each asserting exact token + index + message. Pure module, no UI integration. Vitest: 6 → 33 (all green, 130ms). Build + new-file lint clean. **Deviation:** `pnpm lint` reports a pre-existing `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` error in `StepControls.tsx` (lands in commit `2d2d73b`, before this block). Not introduced by this commit; left for a follow-up since the plan forbids modifying components in commit 2.
- **Commit 3 landed (`542cf3c`).** `web/src/components/ScrambleControls.tsx` replaces `ScrambleButton.tsx` (deleted). Slider 1–30 + numeric readout (`length: N`) + Scramble button + Surprise Me button. Internal `length` state initial 14 (matches old hardcoded value). Test IDs preserved for M9.1 e2e back-compat (`scramble-button` still works for the existing real-backend spec); new IDs `length-slider`, `length-readout`, `surprise-button`. Surprise Me picks `1 + Math.floor(Math.random() * 30)`, updates the slider, then immediately fires `onScramble(rand)`. App.tsx wiring unchanged — `handleScramble(length)` already accepted the param. E2E extended with a second `test()` in the same describe: network-spy on `**/api/scramble` verifies `body.length === 6` after `slider.fill("6")` (stub-mode solve returns empty moves, so request-payload check is the right surface — we can't infer length from the response). Surprise Me asserts readout matches `^length: (\d+)$` and the integer ∈ [1, 30] — "must change" is not a contract (same-value rolls are valid; ~3% chance of stable). Vitest: 33 unchanged (no new vitest tests this commit). Playwright stub: 3 → 4 passed (+ 2 skipped real-only). Lint + build clean.
- **Commit 4 landed (`9d28c49`).** `web/src/state/validateFacelet.ts` (+ test, 9 cases: SOLVED, post-applyMoves, trim, length errors short/long/empty, charset errors uppercase/lowercase, count error). Pure validator returns `{ ok: true, state } | { ok: false, error }`; charset URFDLB, exactly 9 of each, trim-on-input. `web/src/components/StateField.tsx` (textarea + Copy + Set + inline error; key-remount on scrambleState change via parent's `key={scrambleState}`). `web/src/components/MovesField.tsx` (textarea + Copy + Set + inline error; key-remount on solution change; uses `parseMoves`/`formatMoves`/`ParseMovesError` from commit 2; clipboard failures swallowed silently since some browser contexts disallow it). `App.tsx` adds `handleSetState` (clears solution, resets stepIdx) and `handleSetMoves` (resets stepIdx; leaves scrambleState alone) — layout this commit is a simple flex-row below the scramble/solve buttons; commit 5 owns the final reflow. 5 new Playwright stub specs cover clipboard round-trip (with `context.grantPermissions(["clipboard-read","clipboard-write"])`), paste-state→solved-cube (asserts all 6 face centers + 9-of-each conservation), paste-invalid→error (53-char input → "expected 54"), paste-valid-moves→MoveList (5 items, indexed text checks), paste-invalid-moves→error ("Invalid move 'R2'"). Vitest: 33 → 42 passed. Playwright stub: 4 → 9 passed (+ 2 skipped real-only). Lint + build clean.
- **Commit 5 landed (`713dac1`).** `web/src/components/MoveStripView.tsx` — primary visualization. 6-col CSS grid; each cell `[move-label, 80px cube, step#]`; click-to-jump via `onJumpTo→setStepIdx`; active cell has 2px solid #4299ff border + bold label; states memoized via `useMemo` on `[scrambleState, solution]` so stepIdx ticks don't recompute the grid. `web/src/components/FlatCubeRenderer.tsx` gained an optional `testId` prop (default `"flat-cube"`, pass `null` to omit) so strip cells don't collide with the existing `flat-cube` testid contract — secondary big cube keeps the testid (renderer/scramble-solve/state-moves-paste/step-through specs unchanged); strip cells suppress. `web/preview/strip-view.html` — static HTML preview (favorite pattern). Three demos (length 0, 14 with active step 5, 30 with active step 22) × light+dark; inlines `FACELET_MOVES` table verbatim + `applyMove(s)` for pure-static SVG generation; intentional duplication flagged in the file header. `App.tsx` reflowed: state+moves fields top, scramble+solve below, `MoveStripView` primary main, animation player (`<h2>animation player</h2>` + 160px FlatCubeRenderer + MoveList + StepControls) demoted secondary. `maxWidth` bumped 720→1100 to fit the strip at length 14. Real-backend e2e `web/e2e/strip-view.spec.ts` covers scramble length 8 → solve → strip renders N+1 cells → click cell 4 → step counter syncs `4 / N` → active highlight flips. Vitest: 42 unchanged. Playwright stub: 9 → 9 passed (3 skipped real-only describes — strip-view + step-through + scramble-solve real).
- **Strip view layout iteration (V2 in-SVG labels) + size selector landed (`233bf3a`).** Visual eyeball on the static preview surfaced two issues with the original commit-5 layout: the external label/step rows wasted vertical space; centered labels didn't visually align with the cube geometry. Iterated on `web/preview/strip-view.html` through 5 placement variants (V0 baseline + V1–V4 corner variants), then refined the winning V2 (move bottom-left at `(10·stickerPx, 2·stickerPx)`, step# bottom-left at `(0.5·stickerPx, 8.075·stickerPx)`, fonts `1.2·`/`0.85·stickerPx`, cell 0 has no move-label). Cube grid dropped from 6→5 cols (length-14 packs into 3 rows of 5 exactly). `FlatCubeRenderer` gained an `overlays?: ReadonlyArray<Overlay>` prop with an exported `OverlayBaseline` union typed against React's strict `dominantBaseline` JSX type (couldn't ship plain `string`). `MoveStripView` now renders cells via that prop; external label/step `<span>`s deleted; `cubeSize` prop added. App.tsx adds a Small/Medium/Large segmented control (80/120/160 px, default Medium) above the strip with test IDs `strip-size-{small,medium,large}`. Vitest: 42 unchanged. Playwright stub: 9 → 9 passed.
- **Pre-existing M9.1 lint fix (`6ec128d`, drive-by from commit 2's lint sweep).** `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` error in `StepControls.tsx:38` (auto-stop effect calling `setPlaying` synchronously in effect body). Folded the auto-stop into the interval callback (setState in async callback is fine); dropped the second effect. Caught by the close-time `pnpm lint` over the whole tree, surfaced by commit 2 since pre-M9.1 the project never ran ESLint over the post-M9.1 component set.
- **Final close-time verification sweep.** `uv run pytest`: 707 passed, 1 deselected, 34.92s. `uv run pytest -m slow`: 1 passed, 707 deselected, 1.66s. `pnpm --prefix web test` (vitest): 42 passed, 121ms. `pnpm --prefix web test:e2e` (Playwright stub): 9 passed + 3 skipped (real-only), 1.4s. `PLAYWRIGHT_REAL_BACKEND=1 pnpm --prefix web test:e2e`: 12 passed (9 stub + 3 real-only including `step-through.spec.ts` and the new `strip-view.spec.ts`), 12.3s. `uv run ruff check src/ tests/ scripts/codegen/`: clean. `pnpm --prefix web lint`: clean.

**Outcome:**
- **Block goal met.** Demo is now an inspection tool, not just a demonstration. User flow: pick a scramble length on the slider (1–30) or hit Surprise Me → cube renders → optionally paste a known state or move sequence → click Solve → the strip view shows every intermediate state in a 5-col grid with the move-label and step# inside each cube SVG; click any strip cell to jump to that step → animation player below replays smoothly. All paths verified by Playwright against both stub-net and the real LN model.
- **Decisions worth recording:**
  - **Hand-roll move parser over `Object.keys(FACELET_MOVES)`, not cubing.js's `Alg`.** Single source of truth — the parser can't drift from the move table the network was trained on. Training is QTM-only so we'd reject `R2`/`Rw`/`M`/`x` anyway; cubing.js's broader parser would have been overkill plus a coupling we don't need.
  - **Codegen-time validation over consumer-side checks (re-affirmed from M9.1).** `validateFacelet` is a pure module with structural checks (length, charset, exactly-9-of-each); no cube-state reachability check (would be a bigger algebraic test). Catches paste typos without policing what's algebraically a valid cube position — those land harmlessly because the renderer only cares about the 54-char string.
  - **Visual iteration via the static HTML preview was decisive.** Five label-placement variants got compared on `web/preview/strip-view.html` before any React change. The winning V2 (move bottom-left of text aligned with col 10 / row 1 bottom; step# bottom-left of cube at ½-cubie pad) was tweaked through 3 micro-iterations of the static file (centered → corner-padded right-anchored → left-anchored at col 10) BEFORE touching the TSX. Validates the "favorite pattern" — visual issues caught in `open <file>` instead of in the live demo's restart loop.
  - **`FlatCubeRenderer.overlays` prop, not `MoveStripView` rendering its own SVG.** Considered duplicating the SVG generation in MoveStripView so it could overlay text — would have reduced FlatCubeRenderer's surface area but at the cost of duplicating the 12-face rect-rendering loop. The optional `overlays` prop on FlatCubeRenderer keeps a single SVG-rendering codepath; non-strip consumers pass nothing and get the original behavior.
  - **`OverlayBaseline` exported as a strict union, not `string`.** Forced by React's JSX types — `dominantBaseline` rejects free-form strings. Harmless: only `central` (default) and `text-after-edge` (move-label) are used, and the union enumerates them all.
  - **Strip cells use `testId={null}` on FlatCubeRenderer to suppress the `data-testid="flat-cube"`.** Otherwise a strip of 15 cells would each carry the testid, breaking every existing e2e spec that uses `getByTestId("flat-cube")` (singular). Option A (configurable testid prop) won over option B (rewrite all the existing e2e selectors) — kept the M9.1 contract intact.
  - **5-column strip grid (down from the planned 6).** Length-14 packs into 3 rows of 5 exactly; length-30 packs into 6 rows of 5 + 1. Aesthetically the 6-col layout left an awkward partial row at length 14. The static-HTML iteration surfaced this immediately.
  - **`key` trick for prop-driven textarea reset.** StateField uses `key={scrambleState}`, MovesField uses `key={solution.join(" ")}` to force component remount when the parent's source-of-truth changes. Idiomatic React 19 way to reset internal `useState` without `useEffect`-sync (which would trip `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect`). Pre-existing instance of that lint rule in M9.1's `StepControls.tsx` got fixed in `6ec128d` along the way (auto-stop folded into the interval callback).
- **Followups (not done in this block — still M9.3 territory):**
  - Mobile / touch-friendly responsive layout.
  - URL-encoded shareable state (`/#state=...` or `/#scramble=...`).
  - Manual color-grid entry (six 3×3 sticker grids, click to recolor).
  - Animation playback speed slider (current 500ms is hardcoded in `StepControls.tsx`).
  - 2x2 cube exposed in the UI (backend stays parameterized; M9 explicitly scopes UI to 3x3 only).
- **Process notes:**
  - Subagent-per-commit cadence held (parallels M9.1). Five general-purpose dispatches handled commits 2–5 + the V2/size-selector iteration; main thread orchestrated, dispatched the visual iteration on the static HTML inline (since visual feedback is fast and main-thread-bound), and ran the close-time verification sweep.
  - Visual iteration loop used `open web/preview/strip-view.html` × N, with edits to the static HTML between reloads. Three roundtrips converged on V2 with the right paddings, fonts, and corner anchors. Total visual loop time: ~5 minutes. The same iteration in the live React demo would have been ~3–4× slower (rebuild + Vite HMR on every change + manual click-through to set up the post-solve state to inspect).

**Commits:** `7f1bd64` (open), `803e43e` (plan), `52f1672` (log amend), `9ae2bd2` (parser+formatter), `d0cd95b` (log amend), `6ec128d` (drive-by lint fix on StepControls), `542cf3c` (length picker), `1d1bdbe` (log amend), `9d28c49` (state/moves paste fields), `8a29b1c` (log amend), `713dac1` (strip view + animation-player demote), `30b42c2` (log amend), `233bf3a` (V2 in-SVG labels + size selector), `<this commit>` (close).

## 2026-05-08 23:12 — M9.1 solver demo MVP ✅ done — Web demo MVP working: scramble → trained LN net solves → animated step-through; all e2e + unit + integration tests green
**Goal:** Web UI MVP that demonstrates the trained 3x3 ValueNet solving cubes. Random-scramble button → flat sideways-cross 2D render → Solve button → display moves → step-through animation. FastAPI backend wraps existing `beam_solve_batch`; React + TS + Vite frontend; cubing.js for notation parsing and scramble generation (and `<twisty-player>` later in M9.2). First block of M9 — pivot from M8 (we have a usable trained model: `ln_kmax30_100k`, d=14=0.98, d=30=0.76 at width=256 BF16).
**Milestone:** M9.1 — first block under the [M9 plan](plans/m9-ui.md) ([roadmap entry](ROADMAP.md#milestones))
**Approach:** Branch `m9.1-solver-demo` from main HEAD `fa25ebe`. Two parallel scaffolds (per [`plans/m9-ui.md`](plans/m9-ui.md) "M9.1 — Solver demo MVP"):
- **Backend** at `src/rubik/server/`. FastAPI app with lifespan that loads `experiments/davi-3x3/runs/20260508T084940Z_ln_kmax30_100k/net_final.pt` and warms one solve before the server reports healthy. Endpoints: `POST /api/scramble`, `POST /api/solve`, `GET /api/health`. Optional-dep group `[server]` in `pyproject.toml` for `fastapi` + `uvicorn`. `rubik-serve` script entry-point. CORS open to `http://localhost:5173` in dev.
- **Frontend** at `web/`. Vite + React + TS scaffold with pnpm. cubing.js npm dep. Components: `FlatCubeRenderer` (SVG sideways-cross), `ScrambleButton`, `SolveButton`, `MoveList`, `StepControls`. cubing.js applies moves client-side from facelet state.

Detailed task breakdown lands in `plans/m9.1-solver-demo.md` when execution starts (post-/clear) — written via plan mode before any implementation commits.
**Next:** Block closed. Demo working end-to-end on this machine; `m9.1-solver-demo` branch ready to merge. M9.2 (3D rendering via cubing.js `<twisty-player>`) and M9.3 (paste-notation + mobile + share URL) carry forward per `plans/m9-ui.md`.
**In progress:**
- Block opened. Branch `m9.1-solver-demo` from main HEAD `fa25ebe`. Milestone plan landed at `plans/m9-ui.md` (commit `fa25ebe`); ROADMAP M9 entry replaced (was placeholder, now planned with sub-phase breakdown); kociemba comparison added as backlog item.
- **Plan landed at `plans/m9.1-solver-demo.md`.** Eight atomic commits: (1) facelet ↔ kociemba converter, (2) FastAPI skeleton with stub endpoints + tests, (3) real model load + warmup + handlers, (4) Vite/React/TS/cubing.js scaffold, (5) flat sideways-cross renderer + static HTML preview, (6) scramble + solve flow wired, (7) step controls + facelet-move codegen, (8) close. Sticker layout confirmed: kociemba conversion is a face-block permutation `(U,L,F,R,B,D)→(U,R,F,D,L,B)` over the existing row-major within-face order; no existing converter found. Single-row N=1 path through `beam_solve_batch` will be exercised in production for the first time.
- **Commit 1 landed (`cceb0b6`).** `src/rubik/notation/facelet.py` + `tests/notation/test_facelet.py` (+31 tests, suite 669 → 700, 36.8s green). Pure converter; permutation derived at call time from `spec.faces` against module-level `_KOCIEMBA_FACE_ORDER = ("U","R","F","D","L","B")`. Generalizes across `CUBE_2X2` and `CUBE_3X3`. Subagent flagged that the plan-body line describing the index permutation as `(0,1,2,3,4,5)→(0,3,2,1,4,5)` is wrong (correct is `(0,4,2,1,5,3)`); since the implementation derives from `spec.faces` it never used the wrong constant — plan markdown will be corrected in the next plan-revision commit.
- **Plan amended for browser-driven verification.** Per user feedback, Playwright e2e folds into commits 4–8 to replace manual click-throughs: chromium-only project, `webServer` array boots both uvicorn + vite, `RUBIK_USE_STUB_NET=1` lifespan path skips the 234M model load for fast UI tests, `PLAYWRIGHT_REAL_BACKEND=1` opt-in runs the same flow against the real LN model.
