Manual eyeball stays for commit 5's static HTML preview only (aesthetic judgement). Reaffirmed pnpm-only — never `npm`/`npx`.
- **Commit 2 landed (`f81d48d`).** FastAPI skeleton with stub endpoints + tests. `[project.optional-dependencies] server` group adds `fastapi>=0.115` + `uvicorn[standard]>=0.30`; `[project.scripts]` adds `rubik-serve = "rubik.server.app:run"`; `httpx>=0.27` joins dev deps for `TestClient`. CORS allowlist for `http://localhost:5173`. Six endpoint tests, all green. TestClient must run as a context manager to fire the FastAPI lifespan — switched to a `scope="module"` fixture. Test count: 700 → 706.
- **Commit 3 landed (`e0f28fa`).** Real model load + warmup + handler wiring. `inference.load_model` reads sibling `config.yaml` for arch (`body_widths=(5120,1024)`, `n_residual_blocks=4`, `normalization=ln`), instantiates `ValueNet`, loads bundle (`net_state` preferred over bare state-dict, mirroring `scripts/beam_eval_model.py:91-103`). `solve_facelet` runs `beam_solve_batch` with N=1 — first production exercise of the cross-scramble batched shape at single-state. `RUBIK_USE_STUB_NET=1` lifespan branch installs a zero-returning `nn.Module` (with a dummy `nn.Parameter` so `next(net.parameters()).device` works) and skips MPS warmup — consumed by Playwright in commit 4. `pyyaml` joins `[project.optional-dependencies] server` (not dev — runs in server lifespan). `pytest.mark.slow` registered with `addopts = "-m 'not slow'"` so default suite stays fast. Real-model integration test wall time: ~0.9s.
- **Commit 4 landed (`abc532a`).** Vite 8.0.11 + React 19.2.6 + TS 6.0.3 + cubing.js 0.63.3 + Playwright 1.59.1 — pnpm-only (lock committed). `web/playwright.config.ts` `webServer` array boots both processes (uvicorn with `RUBIK_USE_STUB_NET=1` by default; `PLAYWRIGHT_REAL_BACKEND=1` opts into real model). Two non-obvious gotchas: (a) `vite.config.ts` must bind `127.0.0.1` (not `localhost`) — Playwright's `baseURL` is IPv4-only on macOS 25.3 / Vite-on-localhost binds IPv6-first; (b) `webServer.env` strips parent env unless you spread `{ ...process.env, RUBIK_USE_STUB_NET: "1" }`, otherwise `uv` isn't on PATH for the spawned backend.
- **Commit 5 landed (`c056018`).** Flat sideways-cross renderer + static HTML preview + Playwright structural test. `FlatCubeRenderer.tsx` renders 54 `<rect>`s with `data-pos="0..53"` + `data-color="U|R|F|D|L|B"` (Playwright contract). `web/preview/flat-cube.html` is the user's "favorite pattern" — pure-static HTML duplicating the SVG generation logic with three facelets (solved + length-7 + length-20) at three sizes (96/192/384px) × two themes; opens with `open` for human aesthetic eyeball. Renderer e2e asserts 54 rects, 9 of each color, and each face's center sticker matches the canonical solved color.
- **Commit 6 landed (`2f9c6ba`).** Scramble + solve flow wired. `web/src/api/client.ts` is hand-written typed `fetch` wrappers (the schemas are simple enough not to warrant codegen). Three new components (`ScrambleButton`, `SolveButton`, `MoveList`) with `data-testid` hooks. App refactor: `cubeState` initially `SOLVED_3X3`, hoisted post-scramble, cleared on solve. Playwright e2e (stub-mode) clicks Scramble, polls until at least one canonical corner sticker shifts, clicks Solve, asserts `MoveList` mounts. Real-backend describe block skipped under `PLAYWRIGHT_REAL_BACKEND` env unset.
- **Commit 7 landed (`2d2d73b`).** Step-through controls + facelet-move codegen. The substantive piece: `scripts/codegen/dump_facelet_moves.py` derives per-move 54-index permutation tables by composing the internal `apply_moves` permutation with the kociemba face permutation, and emits `web/src/state/faceletMoves.ts`. **Sanity check inside the codegen** verifies each derived perm round-trips against `state_to_facelet(apply_moves(...))` before write — catches compose-direction bugs at write time, not runtime. `applyMove` / `applyMoves` consume the table client-side so step-through doesn't round-trip per click. `StepControls.tsx` exposes Reset / Prev / Play / Next + a "stepIdx / total" counter; play interval = 500ms (2 moves/sec). App now derives `displayedState = applyMoves(scrambleState, solution.slice(0, stepIdx) as MoveStr[])` via `useMemo`. Vitest setup: 6 unit tests for `applyMove` (length preservation, X·X' = id, X⁴ = id, sticker-count conservation under length-14 scramble, bad-input rejection). `tests/server/test_facelet_moves_codegen.py` is the snapshot guard — re-runs the codegen via subprocess in `--check` mode and fails if `web/src/state/faceletMoves.ts` has drifted. Real-backend Playwright spec (`web/e2e/step-through.spec.ts`) exercises the FULL demo: scramble → real solve → play → cube reaches solved (all 6 face centers + 9-of-each conservation).
- **Pre-close fix (`287a2af`).** First close-attempt verification sweep caught a real test-isolation bug from commit 3: `tests/server/test_endpoints.py` set `RUBIK_USE_STUB_NET=1` at module-import time, which fired during pytest collection of the slow suite and contaminated `test_inference_integration.py` (which expects the env var UNSET to load the real LN model). Fix: move the env-var set into the `client` fixture body with try/finally cleanup that restores the prior value. Both default and slow suites green afterward. Lesson: module-level `os.environ` writes are a pytest-collection hazard — fixtures or `monkeypatch` are the right tools.
- **Final verification sweep (this commit).** Default `uv run pytest`: 707 passed, 1 deselected, 34.98s. Slow `uv run pytest -m slow`: 1 passed, 707 deselected, 0.85s. Vitest: 6 passed, 108ms. Playwright stub `pnpm test:e2e`: 3 passed (+ 2 skipped real-only describes), 2.2s. Playwright real backend `PLAYWRIGHT_REAL_BACKEND=1 pnpm test:e2e`: 5 passed (the 3 stub-also specs run again under real backend, plus the 2 real-only specs including step-through), 11.4s. Ruff clean across `src/ tests/ scripts/codegen/`. (Pre-existing ruff failures in `experiments/davi-2x2/analysis/` from a prior block remain — drive-by backlog item, not addressed here.)

**Outcome:**
- **Block goal met.** Web UI MVP working end-to-end. User clicks *Scramble (length 14)* → flat 2D render shows the scrambled cube; clicks *Solve* → trained LN net (`ln_kmax30_100k`) returns a solution in ~0.3-1s; clicks *Play* → cube animates step-by-step back to solved at 2 moves/sec. All paths verified by Playwright against both the stub-net (fast UI deterministic) and real LN model (headline guard) backends.
- **The single-row N=1 path through `beam_solve_batch` is exercised in production for the first time.** Cross-scramble-batched implementation handles the degenerate single-state case cleanly; no special path needed.
- **Decisions worth recording:**
  - **Optional-dependency group `[project.optional-dependencies] server` (PEP 631 extras), not `[dependency-groups]` (PEP 735).** Extras are friendlier for `pip install rubik[server]` consumers; PEP 735 groups would be cleaner under `uv sync --group server` but lose pip-extras compatibility. Installed via `uv sync --extra server` throughout.
  - **`pyyaml` in `[project.optional-dependencies] server`, not `[dependency-groups] dev`.** `inference._read_arch` is prod code that runs in the server lifespan, not dev tooling.
  - **Codegen as snapshot, NOT as build step.** `scripts/codegen/dump_facelet_moves.py` is a one-shot Python script; `web/src/state/faceletMoves.ts` is committed; `tests/server/test_facelet_moves_codegen.py` re-runs the script in `--check` mode and snapshot-compares. Reasoning: making `pnpm dev` Python-aware would couple the frontend to the Python toolchain unnecessarily. The snapshot-as-test pattern catches drift without coupling build pipelines. Internal cube tables are stable; the test will fire on the rare day someone touches them.
  - **Stub-net + real-backend split for Playwright.** `RUBIK_USE_STUB_NET=1` (default in `webServer.env`) installs a zero-returning `nn.Module` and skips MPS warmup — fast deterministic UI tests. `PLAYWRIGHT_REAL_BACKEND=1` flips to the real LN model for the headline solve-correctness guard. The split makes the default fast-suite work in CI without an MPS-equipped runner while still keeping the real-model exercise in the engineer's local loop.
  - **Vite host explicit (`127.0.0.1`).** Playwright's `baseURL` is IPv4-only on this stack; default Vite-on-`localhost` binds IPv6-first and Playwright connection-refuses. Documented in `vite.config.ts`.
  - **`webServer.env` requires `{ ...process.env, ... }`.** Playwright strips parent env when `env` is set; without inheriting PATH, the spawned `uv run rubik-serve` can't find `uv`. Captured in `web/playwright.config.ts`.
  - **Sanity-check inside the codegen, not at consumer site.** Catches compose-direction errors at write time. The TS consumer (`applyMove.ts`) trusts the table; the test guards against drift; the codegen guards against incorrect generation.
  - **Module-level `os.environ` writes are a pytest-collection hazard.** Caught only by the close's full-suite verification sweep (commit 3 had been reviewed in isolation). Fix: env-var sets belong inside fixtures with try/finally cleanup, or use `monkeypatch`. Recorded for future test work.
- **Followups (not done in this block):**
  - **Pre-existing ruff failures in `experiments/davi-2x2/analysis/` (~23 errors, 4 files).** Surfaced by the agent in commit 2, not introduced by M9.1. Worth a small drive-by cleanup block; not blocking.
  - **`final_value` field in `SolveStats` is currently always `0.0`.** `BeamSearchResult` doesn't surface the final-step V; would require a small extension to `beam_solve_batch`'s return type. Not blocking the demo. Park for M9.2 or later.
  - **Scramble length is hardcoded to 14 in `ScrambleButton`.** A length-picker UI is M9.3 polish.
  - **Step-through play speed is hardcoded to 500ms (2 moves/sec).** A speed slider is also M9.3 polish.
- **Process notes:**
  - Each commit went out via a single `general-purpose` subagent dispatch with a self-contained brief — main thread stayed focused on review and orchestration. The CLAUDE.md "delegate to subagents" rule made this work; running 9 commits inline would have blown out context.
  - Plan amendment mid-block (Playwright addition, commit `210b43e`) was the right call once user requirements shifted — kept the plan file as ground truth rather than executing against a stale brief.
  - Final verification sweep caught a real bug (the env-leak in commit 3) that earlier per-commit verification missed. The "run all surfaces in one go before close" gate earned its keep.

**Commits:** `1152989` (open — pre-block), `3cb0d7c` (plan), `cceb0b6` (facelet), `210b43e` (plan amend — Playwright), `f81d48d` (server skeleton), `e0f28fa` (server real wiring + stub-net), `abc532a` (web scaffold + Playwright), `c056018` (flat renderer + preview + e2e), `2f9c6ba` (scramble/solve flow), `2d2d73b` (step-through + codegen), `287a2af` (test-isolation fix), `<this commit>` (close).

## 2026-05-08 — LN/K_max=30 fresh training run (30k → 100k via warm-start) ✅ done — both runs trained healthily; eval tooling refactored mid-block; full re-eval under fast_kmax30 shows d=14 saturates by ~step 40k, d=30=0.76 at end-of-training
**Goal:** Real comparison run for the LN+K_max=30 path. The pre-flight smoke (LOG entry below) ran 5k steps under this config and showed an unexpectedly favorable trajectory: macro_v_star_mae 2.29 → 0.18 by step 5k, d=14=0.30 — vs full_train (BN, K_max=20, no curriculum) at d=14≈0 at the same step count and d=14=0.78 only at step 16k. 30k steps lets us see whether the curve sustains, whether LN's claimed drift-resistance shows up at scale, and whether the d=14 capability climb past 5k matches or exceeds what BN+K_max=20 produced over a longer horizon. Out of scope: comparison against curriculum-enabled variants (separate cycle).
**Milestone:** M8 phase 3 — first non-smoke run on the LN line ([roadmap entry](ROADMAP.md#m8))
**Approach:** Branch `ln-kmax30-fresh-run` from main HEAD `a34b560`. Single deliverable: a 30k-step fresh-init run with `experiments/davi-3x3/configs/ln_kmax30.yaml` (mirrors smoke_ln_kmax30.yaml, only `n_steps` differs: 10000 → 30000). wandb on (per the just-locked-in default). Checkpoint cadence stays at 500 steps (60 checkpoints). Use `ensure_run_results.py` periodically during the run to keep eval reports current — the new tooling enables this. After training completes (or is killed early), final eval pass + writeup of comparison vs full_train at matched step counts.
**Next:** Block closed. User pivoting to other work; the unfinished comparison writeup vs full_train (and adding fast_kmax30 trajectory data to `error_trajectories.html`) carries forward as followups noted in **Outcome**. Open a new block when the next goal crystallizes.

**In progress:**
- Block opened. Branch `ln-kmax30-fresh-run` from main HEAD `a34b560`. Config landed at `experiments/davi-3x3/configs/ln_kmax30.yaml` (30k).
- **30k training kicked off** (run dir `20260508T054741Z_ln_kmax30`, wandb URL `https://wandb.ai/griffin-enterprises/rubik-3x3/runs/xwrujaup`). Healthy at step 21700/30000 — loss bouncing around 0.19-0.22 (deep K_max=30 targets are harder than smoke's K_max≤8 ones, that's expected); macro_v_star_mae 0.096 at step 21500 — calibration improving steadily.
- **Scope expanded: chained 100k continuation for overnight.** Per user, after 30k completes the chainer warm-starts a 70k continuation (config `ln_kmax30_100k.yaml`, n_steps=100000, checkpoint_every=5000). Chainer at `/tmp/ln_kmax30_chain.sh` (PID 73956, detached via nohup): polls for 30k's net_final.pt, runs ensure_run_results on 30k, kicks off the warm-start, then ensure_run_results on the continuation. End-to-end chain ~6.5h after the 30k finishes.
- **Both training runs completed cleanly overnight.** 30k final macro_v_star_mae 0.055; 100k continuation final 0.018. Chainer rendered eval reports under fast.yaml (then length-14, d=14) — the user noticed the eval coverage was capped at d=14 even though training was at K_max=30, kicking off a tooling refactor day.
- **Eval-tooling refactor (committed, 4 commits).** Substantive scope expansion to make the eval pipeline competent for K_max-aware analysis, ensure-by-default with stride control, and partial-run safety:
  - **YAML configs.** `scripts/eval-configs/fast.yaml` and `default.yaml` bumped from length-14 to length-18 (sensible default just past the prior K=14 era). New `scripts/eval-configs/fast_kmax30.yaml` (length-30) for explicit deep eval.
  - **Auto-derive eval depth.** `beam_eval_run.py` and `beam_eval_model.py` now compute `effective_max_depth = min(yaml_n_per_depth_length, training_max_scramble_depth)`. Source for training depth: `metrics.jsonl` `run_start` event preferred, falls back to `config.yaml`. Logs the source/values. Explicit `--max-depth N` bypasses the min.
  - **Renderer.** `scripts/render_beam_eval_report.py` `TRAJECTORY_BANDS` is now dynamic — `trajectory_bands(max_depth)` returns width-5 bands derived from data: d=14 → 5/5/4 (legacy preserved), d=18 → 5/5/5/3, d=30 → 6×5.
  - **`render_error_trajectories.py` RUNS.** Appended `("ln_kmax30 30k ...", "20260508T054741Z_ln_kmax30", "#9467bd", "")` and `("ln_kmax30 100k continuation ...", "20260508T084940Z_ln_kmax30_100k", "#8c564b", "")`. HTML regenerated.
  - **`beam_eval_run.py` workflow refactor.** Renamed `--every-steps` → `--stride`. Default behavior changed from "redo all" to **ensure-by-default** (skip steps with an existing record in the target JSONL). New `--force` flag for explicit recompute. **Largest-step-first traversal** (net_final.pt first, then descending net_step_*.pt) so partial kills leave the most-trained checkpoints already on disk.
  - **Per-checkpoint flush.** Critical bug fix discovered mid-day: the prior writer buffered all records and wrote once at end-of-run, defeating the largest-first design. Now each checkpoint completion writes the rollup. New test `test_jsonl_flushed_per_checkpoint`.
  - **Schema check on read.** Existing JSONL records' `(max_depth, n_per_depth, beam_width, precision, seed)` signature is validated against the current invocation. Mismatch without `--force` → error with both signatures printed. `--force` always backs up the existing JSONL to `beam_eval_<config>.jsonl.bak.<UTC-timestamp>` before starting fresh. Four new tests.
  - **`ensure_run_results.py` is now a thin shim** delegating to `beam_eval_run.py` (kept because `/tmp/ln_kmax30_chain.sh` still calls it; can be deleted once that script is gone).
  - **Test count: 642 → 669** (+27 net across the day).
- **Backlog item added** (`ROADMAP.md`): drive-by rename `beam_eval_*` → `eval_*` since "beam" is implied. Surface area covers all four scripts + the JSONL/HTML filenames in run-dir results.
- **Re-eval pass under new tooling (post-/clear, post-handoff).** With the eval-tooling refactor committed, ran `beam_eval_run.py --config fast_kmax30 --stride 10000 --render-html` on both run dirs. Per-checkpoint flush + largest-first traversal worked as designed: `net_final.pt` for 100k landed in 238.8s (d=30=0.760). 100k re-eval: 7/7 records, ~2400s total. 30k re-eval: 3/3 records, ~1360s total. Both `eval_trajectory_fast_kmax30.html` reports rendered. **Trajectory data:**

  | step    | d=14  | d=20  | d=25  | d=30  |
  |--------:|------:|------:|------:|------:|
  | 10000   | 0.620 | 0.580 | 0.400 | 0.440 |
  | 20000   | 0.800 | 0.740 | 0.520 | 0.780 |
  | 30000   | 0.880 | 0.740 | 0.660 | 0.660 |
  | 40000   | 0.980 | 0.740 | 0.680 | 0.680 |
  | 50000   | 0.980 | 0.680 | 0.740 | 0.660 |
  | 60000   | 0.980 | 0.720 | 0.720 | 0.740 |
  | 70000   | 0.980 | 0.740 | 0.780 | 0.720 |
  | 80000   | 0.980 | 0.780 | 0.720 | 0.780 |
  | 90000   | 0.940 | 0.840 | 0.800 | 0.740 |
  | 100000  | 0.980 | 0.780 | 0.800 | 0.760 |

  Quick read: d=14 saturates by step 40k (≥0.94 thereafter); deeper-V* capability (d=20/25/30) keeps climbing through step 100k. The 30k → 40k jump on d=14 (0.88 → 0.98) coincides with the warm-start boundary; deeper depths' moves are below the noise floor (n=50 gives SE ±6.5pp at p≈0.7).

**Outcome:**
- **Block goal met across both training runs and the eval pass.** 30k fresh-init run reached macro_v_star_mae 0.055 (final), 100k continuation reached 0.018. Both ran clean overnight via the chained warm-start. Curve sustained: `d=14 ≥ 0.94` from step 40k onward, deeper depths still rising at step 100k.
- **Substantive scope expansion: eval-tooling refactor.** Initially scoped as a "real comparison run" block, it absorbed a day of refactor work after the user noticed the chained eval reports were capped at d=14 despite training at K_max=30. Resulted in YAML config layer, K_max-aware auto-derive of eval depth, dynamic trajectory bands (5/5/4 → 6×5 for d=30), `--stride` + ensure-by-default + `--force` semantics, per-checkpoint flush (load-bearing for safe long evals), schema check on JSONL read, and the renderer dynamic-bands path. Test count `642 → 669` (+27).
- **Decisions worth recording:**
  - **Per-checkpoint flush is non-negotiable for long eval passes.** The original buffered-write design lost data on kill. The fix (flush after each checkpoint) plus largest-first traversal means a kill at any time leaves the most-trained checkpoints already on disk. This pattern is now the contract; the schema-check on read enforces consistency across resumes.
  - **`fast_kmax30` (length-30 walks, n=50/depth) is the right cycle-screening config for K_max=30 training.** SE ±6.5pp at deep tail (p≈0.7) is wide but cheap; pairs with `default` (length-18, n=100, SE ±4.6pp) or a future thorough-K_max30 variant for milestone-quality.
  - **Run-dir results/ files are committed alongside the run.** Following the prior block's pattern (.pt files gitignored, JSONL + HTML tracked).
- **Followups (user pivoting; not done in this block):**
  - **Comparison writeup vs full_train at matched step counts.** The block's original ambition. Eval data is in hand for ln_kmax30; full_train was last evaluated under fast.yaml (length-14) only — re-eval under fast_kmax30 is a prerequisite for an apples-to-apples deep comparison. Pick up when returning.
  - **`error_trajectories.html` under fast_kmax30.** The renderer's RUNS table already lists both ln_kmax30 runs (committed `6a779e9`), but the cross-run aggregate plot consumes fast.yaml (length-14). Adding fast_kmax30 (length-30) coverage means either extending `render_error_trajectories.py` to multi-config or adding a sibling `render_error_trajectories_kmax30.py`. Not urgent — single-run trajectory HTMLs cover the same data per-run.
  - **Backlog item: rename `beam_eval_*` → `eval_*`** (already in `ROADMAP.md` backlog from prior amend). Touch surface: 4 scripts + JSONL/HTML filenames in run-dir results + tests.
  - **`net_final.pt` always re-eval'd by ensure path** (carried from prior block; not addressed in this re-eval pass either). Cost: at most one redundant eval per ensure call. If annoying, the dict's `step` field can be loaded once to filter.

**Commits:** `7059804` (open), `b45f024` (100k config), `a84f9d9` (amend — 100k scope), `e597997` (eval-config layer), `6a779e9` (renderer + error_trajectories RUNS), `eb2cbe4` (run-dir + model workflow), `5893ecc` (training-run artifacts), `4bb3134` (amend — eval-tooling refactor handoff), `f47a198` (re-eval artifacts: 30k + 100k cont under fast_kmax30), `<this commit>` (close + outcome).

## 2026-05-07 — Checkpoint provenance polish + LN/K_max=30 smoke ✅ done — pipeline ready for fresh 30k LN run; smoke trajectory looks favorable
**Goal:** Get the 3x3 training pipeline ready for a clean LN + K_max=30 fresh run by (a) tightening checkpoint provenance so eval scripts stop parsing filenames for `step` (it's already in the bundle, scripts just don't read it) and adding `wall_time_seconds` (and `wandb_run_id` when active) to the save bundle, and (b) running a 10k-step smoke training with `normalization: ln`, flat `max_scramble_depth: 30` (no curriculum), checkpoint every 500 steps. The smoke validates: new save path emits the right metadata, eval picks it up through the new code path, trajectory HTML renders, and the LN+K_max=30 config doesn't crash or NaN. Capability is not the bar — "didn't crash, files exist, eval reads them, plots look sensible" is the bar. Out of scope: the real ~3h fresh training run (separate block once smoke is clean).
**Milestone:** M8 phase 3 — pre-flight for fresh LN+K_max=30 run
**Approach:** Branch `checkpoint-provenance-smoke` from main HEAD `6f7fbf5`. Two deliverables, sequenced:
- **Part 1 — Provenance polish (code change).** Edit `_save_checkpoint` in `experiments/davi-3x3/run.py` to add `wall_time_seconds` (plus `wandb_run_id` if `wandb.run` is active). Update `scripts/beam_eval_run.py` (trajectory mode) and any other consumer that regexes filenames for step → read `step` from the loaded dict; fall back to filename parse only when the dict has no `step` key (back-compat with bare-state-dict checkpoints from old runs). Tests: bundle-with-step preferred path, bare-dict fallback path, missing-fields tolerated.
- **Part 2 — Smoke validation.** New config `experiments/davi-3x3/configs/smoke_ln_kmax30.yaml`: same arch as `full_train.yaml`, `normalization: ln`, `max_scramble_depth: 30`, `max_scramble_depth_ramp_steps: 0` (no curriculum), `n_steps: 10000`, `checkpoint_every: 500`. Run it. Eval the final checkpoint with `beam_eval_model.py --config fast`. Run trajectory eval on the run dir. Confirm: 20 checkpoint files exist, each has `wall_time_seconds` in the bundle, eval reads `step` from dicts (no filename parsing in the new path), trajectory HTML renders.
**Next:** Open new block for the actual 30k LN+K_max=30 fresh training run (this block was the pre-flight). Kick off with `experiments/davi-3x3/configs/ln_kmax30.yaml` (30k steps, wandb-on, otherwise mirrors the smoke config).
**In progress:**
- Block opened. Branch `checkpoint-provenance-smoke` from main HEAD `6f7fbf5`. Main fast-forwarded to include the closed `beam-solve-perf` block (21 commits).
- **Provenance bundle landed (`8b715a7`).** `_save_checkpoint` accepts optional `wall_time_seconds` + `wandb_run_id` (omitted-key, no None placeholders). `evaluate_checkpoint` surfaces `checkpoint_step`, `checkpoint_wall_time_seconds`, `checkpoint_wandb_run_id` in payloads. `beam_eval_run.py` prefers dict-step over filename. +6 tests, all targeted suite passes.
- **Smoke config landed (`3869088`)** — `smoke_ln_kmax30.yaml`. Then drove the user-flag fix (`8e9e1f3`) — drop `--no-wandb` from recommended invocation; missing wandb runs are invisible to the user's discovery pattern (saved as feedback memory `feedback_wandb_always_on.md`).
- **Smoke ran ~5100 steps, killed early per user.** 10 checkpoints saved (steps 500..5000), all carry `wall_time_seconds` in bundle. Training healthy at kill: macro_v_star_mae 2.29 → 0.18, no NaN under LN+K_max=30 from random init. Substantive: **d=14=0.30 at step 5k under LN+K_max=30 with NO curriculum.** For comparison, full_train (BN, K_max=20, no curriculum, but 16k steps) reached d=14=0.78 at step 16k. Same step count comparison favors LN+K_max=30 — full_train was at d=14≈0 at step 5k.
- **Eval pipeline validated end-to-end on the partial run.** 10 per-checkpoint payloads emitted, trajectory HTML rendered, dict-step path exercised, metadata fields visible in JSON output.
- **User reported reporting issues from inspecting the smoke output.** Awkward "Per-walk-depth" titles, x-axis label collisions in heatmap, truncated run-name legend, flat per-checkpoint JSONs hard to scan. Bundled scope expansion into this block.
- **metrics.jsonl event metadata (`f8d8bc4`).** `run_start` carries `wandb_run_id` (None on --no-wandb, stable schema for analyzers). Periodic `checkpoint` event carries `wall_time_seconds`. The .pt bundle stays the source of truth — this is duplication for IDE-greppability without `torch.load`. +3 tests.
- **JSONL consolidation + renderer polish (`442595a`).** Per-checkpoint JSONs replaced with one consolidated JSONL per config at `<run-dir>/results/beam_eval_<config>.jsonl` (sorted ascending by step, dedup-by-step on re-run). HTML renamed `trajectory_<config>.html` → `eval_trajectory_<config>.html`. Renderer back-compat: reads both jsonl and legacy per-file inputs. Title polish: dropped "Per-walk-depth ×" prefixes and run-name suffixes globally. Y-axis: `walk_depth=N` → `d=N`. Friendly run labels in legend (strip leading `YYYYMMDDTHHMMSSZ_` prefix). Test count `626 → 642`.
- **Idempotent wrapper landed (`ebf184c`)** — `scripts/ensure_run_results.py`. Checks JSONL for already-evaluated steps and only evals the missing ones; always re-renders HTML. Validated empirically on the smoke: all-up-to-date case ~1s (HTML re-render only); one-stale case ~64s (eval just the one missing checkpoint). Distinct from `beam_eval_run.py` which is the explicit "redo from scratch" path. Solves the "kill long training, still get reports" workflow without needing a SIGINT handler.
- **Smoke artifacts + error_trajectories.html regen (`4c34732`).** Smoke run dir committed (config, metrics.jsonl, results/ — .pt files gitignored). Smoke registered in `render_error_trajectories.py` RUNS table; calibration / V*-MAE / pred-drift now show the smoke alongside prior runs.

**Outcome:**
- **Block goal met across both deliverables.** Provenance pipeline (bundle + dict-step in consumers + metrics.jsonl event metadata) is end-to-end working. LN+K_max=30 smoke validates the path AND surfaces a favorable training trajectory worth following up on at scale.
- **Decisions recorded:**
  - **Bundle vs metrics.jsonl as source of truth.** `.pt` bundle is canonical for `step`, `wall_time_seconds`, `wandb_run_id`. metrics.jsonl carries the same fields *also* for IDE-greppability (no `torch.load` needed). Eval consumers read from the bundle. Sibling `config.yaml` stays the source of truth for arch/training-config (NOT duplicated into the bundle, per user's "don't duplicate readily-available data" guidance).
  - **JSONL over per-checkpoint files.** One `beam_eval_<config>.jsonl` per config, dedup-by-step on append. At record sizes ~2 KB now and ~4 KB max under thorough config, jsonl is the right shape — only goofy when records hit MB scale (not us).
  - **Wandb-on-by-default for ALL runs.** Smoke included. Missing wandb runs are invisible to the user's discovery pattern. Saved as `feedback_wandb_always_on.md` in user memory.
  - **Kill-and-render via idempotent wrapper, NOT signal handlers.** `ensure_run_results.py` is the canonical "make my reports current" verb. Same command for in-flight, killed, completed, and stale runs. SIGINT handlers in `run.py` deferred — periodic checkpoints (every 500 steps) limit lost-work blast radius to ~2 min, not worth the complexity.
  - **Curriculum scheduling stays disabled for the LN+K_max=30 line.** "Earn every hyperparameter" — curriculum is unproven on 3x3 and would confound LN's effect. The 30k follow-up keeps `max_scramble_depth_ramp_steps: 0`.
- **Decisions explicitly NOT made:**
  - Whether LN+K_max=30 outperforms BN+K_max=20 long-term. 5k-step partial smoke is suggestive (matches full_train's step-5k macro_v_star_mae and beats it on d=14 capability) but not conclusive. The 30k run is the real comparison.
  - Whether to migrate the warm_continue per-file artifacts to JSONL. Renderer accepts both; if the cost ever matters, a migration script is straightforward.
- **Followups (notes for backlog):**
  - **Centralized run-config-snapshot in bundle?** Currently arch/training config lives in sibling `config.yaml` only. If a checkpoint gets copied without its sibling, eval breaks. User's stance: "fine to look at sibling for now." Revisit if it bites.
  - **2x2 metadata pipeline parity.** This block scoped to 3x3. `experiments/davi-2x2/run.py` still saves bare-state-dict checkpoints in some paths and parses filenames in `analysis/capture_solve_histograms.py`. If 2x2 ever returns to the active surface, mirror the 3x3 changes.
  - **Wrapper extension: error_trajectories regen.** `ensure_run_results.py` currently only handles per-run beam-eval. The error_trajectories.html report is a multi-run aggregate driven by the RUNS table — auto-appending a run to RUNS would be one option, or a complementary `ensure_global_results.py` that triggers the global regens. Deferred: manual edit of RUNS works fine for now.
  - **`net_final.pt` is always re-eval'd by ensure** because its step isn't recoverable from the filename. Cost: at most one redundant eval per ensure call (~50s). If this becomes annoying, ensure could load the bundle's `step` field once to filter.

**Commits:** `c73228c` (open), `8b715a7` (provenance bundle + dict-step), `3869088` (smoke config), `8e9e1f3` (drop --no-wandb default), `f8d8bc4` (metrics.jsonl event metadata), `442595a` (JSONL + renderer polish), `ebf184c` (ensure wrapper), `4c34732` (smoke artifacts + error_trajectories regen), `<this commit>` (close + outcome).

## 2026-05-07 — beam_solve_batch perf overhaul + smart sampling profiles ✅ done — BF16 was the actual lever; rewrite paid for tooling + platform knowledge, not perf
**Goal:** Two coupled deliverables on `beam_eval_walk`'s shared eval+production primitive:
1. **Make `beam_solve_batch` 5–10× faster on M4 Max / MPS** (`src/rubik/search/beam.py`) by eliminating three structural inefficiencies: (a) per-scramble sequential outer loop (1400 independent beam runs today), (b) per-step Python orchestration with GPU↔CPU sync on every step (`.cpu().numpy()` for dedup, `.item()` for early-exit, CPU dict + sorted for top-k), (c) tiny network call batches (~1500 at width=128 instead of ~1M+). Width=8/16 wall-time tie at 30.6s is pure orchestration floor, not compute — orchestration overhaul should remove it.
2. **Smart sampling profiles** that reallocate the eval sample budget away from depths with degenerate state space (d=1 has only 12 reachable walks; n=100 oversamples by 8×) toward depths where binomial SE is the binding constraint (d=11..14 at n=100 has SE ±5pp at p≈0.7). Three named profiles: `fast` (~10s, cycle screening), `default` (~60s, current n=100 uniform), `thorough` (~3min, milestone-quality with n=500 at deep depths). Validated empirically — `fast` must agree within ~2σ of `thorough` on per-cell solve_rate at d=11..14 to be a legitimate cycle-screening proxy.

Both deliverables benefit both eval AND production solve (`beam_solve_batch` is the shared primitive). The perf rewrite is the prerequisite that makes `thorough` affordable. **Out of scope:** training-loop perf (different bottlenecks; gets its own block later).
**Milestone:** M8 phase 3 — perf followup ([prior block](#2026-05-07---beam-eval-as-primary-metric-parameterized-tool--dedicated-html--width-sweep--done) trace established the cost structure)
**Approach:** Branch `beam-solve-perf` from main HEAD `0d2f3f8`. Plan landed at [`plans/m8-beam-solve-perf.md`](plans/m8-beam-solve-perf.md) — six atomic commits:
- **C1 — Cross-scramble batching:** all 1400 scrambles share one beam tensor of shape `(N, beam_width, n_features)`. Forward-pass batch_size = N × width × 12. Outer Python iterations 21k → ~28. Existing tests pass; new test verifies N-invariance.
- **C2 — Run-to-completion (kill `.any().item()`):** drop early-exit, track per-scramble first-solved step on-device. Adds optional per-state `max_steps` (additive API change; uniform remains default). ~10s of GPU↔CPU sync removed.
- **C3 — On-device hash dedup:** state-bytes → int64 hash → `torch.unique` + scatter-min. Drops `.cpu().numpy()` per step. Collision rate ~5×10⁻²⁰ per pair, documented in `state_hash.py`.
- **C4 — GPU top-k:** `torch.topk(values, k=width, largest=False)` replaces CPU `sorted(...)[:width]`.
- **C5 — Equivalence + benchmark:** re-run canonical width sweep, verify per-(depth, width) solve_rate within ±10pp (2σ at n=100, p≈0.7) of baseline `sweep_full_train_final.json`. Aggregate-within-SE only — no cell-by-cell trace matching (statistical algorithm, individual outcomes are noise). Wall gates: ≤2min total, ≤15s/width=128 cell.
- **C6 — Variable n_per_depth + profiles + validation:** `beam_eval_walk` accepts `n_per_depth: int | Sequence[int]`. Three profiles in `experiments/beam-eval-perf/profiles.py`. `--profile fast|default|thorough` CLI flag. Validation experiment: run all three on `full_train net_final.pt`, verify `fast` agrees with `thorough` within `±2 × max(SE_fast, SE_thorough)` at d=11..14. Writeup appends to `results.md` + `intuition.md`.

**Next:** Block closed. Production cycle eval is now `beam_eval_model.py <model.pt> --config default` (~90s for full d=1..14 verdict at width=256 BF16, with auto-render HTML). Open backlog items: chunked-forward at width=512, T0 capacity sweep on 3x3, checkpoint metadata bundle, multi-run trajectory heatmap layout (all in ROADMAP.md).
**In progress:**
- Block opened. Branch `beam-solve-perf` from main HEAD `0d2f3f8`.
- **Plan landed at `plans/m8-beam-solve-perf.md`.** Equivalence stance locked: aggregate-within-SE (no cell-by-cell). Saved a feedback memo (`feedback_aggregate_not_cell_equivalence.md`) for future statistical-algorithm rewrites. Scope expanded after user pushback on the n=100-uniform sampling assumption — d=1 has only 12 reachable walks, n=100 wastes effort there while d=11..14 are SE-bound. C6 (sampling profiles) bundled into this block.
- **C1 landed (`c0b293e`)** — cross-scramble batching. Beam tensor `(N, beam_width, n_stickers)`, net forward batch `N × beam_width × 12` (= 153,600 rows int8 at typical N=100, width=128 shape). Equivalence: **bit-exact** vs baseline at width=128 across all 14 depths (solve_rates match to the third decimal). Test count `21 → 22` (added `test_beam_solve_batch_n_invariant_to_split`). One existing test (`test_n_expansions_counts_per_layer_expansion`) needed an `n_active` counter to preserve "work spent actively solving" semantics — pre-solved rows still get expanded inside the shared tensor, so we decrement `n_active` as rows complete.
- **C1 perf surprise (plan prediction falsified):** width=128 sniff came in at **~116s wall vs ~70s baseline — 1.6× *slower*, not 2-3× faster** as the plan predicted. Cause: the per-scramble Python dedup loop now scales as `N × steps` instead of `N × steps_until_solve`, and the lost early-exit (all N run until LAST scramble solves) compounds it. Forward-pass kernel-launch amortization is real but dominated by the orchestration regression. This is exactly what C2 (kill `.any().item()` sync, run-to-completion) and C3 (on-device hash dedup, drops `.cpu().numpy()` per step) attack — path forward unchanged, but the plan's "C1 alone is 2-3×" mental model was wrong about where the cost lived.
- **C2 landed (`2d0ee4e`)** — run-to-completion, drop `.any().item()` per-step sync, add per-state `max_steps_per_state` kwarg (additive API, scalar `max_steps` remains default). Collision policy: passing both `max_steps` and `max_steps_per_state` raises `ValueError`; passing neither also raises. No silent precedence — surfacing fat-finger errors at the API boundary. Test count `22 → 24` (added `test_beam_solve_batch_no_early_exit_same_solve_lens`, `test_beam_solve_batch_per_state_max_steps`). Equivalence: bit-exact vs C1 across all d=1..14 at width=128. **Perf: width=128 sniff ~167s vs C1's 116s vs baseline 70s — C2 alone is 1.4× SLOWER than C1**, contradicting the plan's "1.5-2× faster" prediction. Mechanism: without early-exit, the Python dedup loop now runs for all 14 max_steps on every cell, including shallow cells where every row solved by step 5-6. Plan's mental model assumed early-exit removal would unlock GPU efficiency; reality is the dominant cost is still the Python dedup loop, and removing early-exit just makes that loop run more times. **Both C1 and C2 are architectural setup costs that C3 attacks wholesale** (replaces the per-scramble Python dedup with `torch.unique` + `scatter_reduce`). MPS quirk check: `torch.where` on bool/int64 works clean, no fallback needed.
- **C3 landed (`2ec4a5d`)** — on-device int64 hash + dedup. New module `src/rubik/search/state_hash.py` (FNV-1a 64-bit, batched ops, MPS-supported). **MPS bug discovered (load-bearing):** `torch.unique` and `torch.sort` on int64 only consider the **low 32 bits** — the initial scramble-id-XOR-into-high-bits dedup scheme silently merged scrambles, dropping solve_rate@d=14 from 0.640 to 0.100. Bisected via small-N solve sweep + direct `torch.unique([1, 1<<32])` probe. Pivoted to **hash-on-GPU + numpy dedup on CPU** over `(N, M)` int64 + float32 (~1.2MB transfer per step at width=128 — 7× less than C2's state tensor, sync still cheap). Other MPS findings: `scatter_reduce_(reduce="amin")` works for float32 but is unsupported for int64; tiebreak idx kept as float32. **All documented in beam.py + state_hash.py docstrings.** Test count `24 → 35` (new `tests/search/test_state_hash.py`: state_hash module + collision-free check on QTM≤4 corpus, 3x3=11,206 states + 2x2=7k states). **Equivalence: bit-exact at every (d=1..14) cell, max delta 0.000.** **Perf: width=128 sniff ~115.1s vs C2's 167s vs baseline 70s — 31% faster than C2, finally moving the right direction.** The Python `sorted()` top-k is now the obvious remaining hotspot, exactly where C4 attacks.
- **C4 landed (`95892a7`)** — `torch.topk(values, k=width, largest=False)` replaces per-row Python `sorted()`. Strategy: explicit `k_eff = min(beam_width, n_children)` clamp + index-tile pad for the short-beam edge case (only triggers at step 0 with cur_beam=1, fan-out=12 — pad slots dedup naturally on next step). **Equivalence: bit-exact at every cell, max delta 0.000.** No tied-V states observed at width=128 on the trained net, so tiebreak rule shift is unobservable. **Perf: width=128 sniff ~111.6s vs C3's 115.1s — only 3% improvement.** Test count `35 → 36`.
- **Profile findings (post-C4, instrumentation since reverted):** the C4 executor's prediction that "remaining CPU cost is the per-row np.unique loop" was **wrong**. Decomposed width=128 cell wall: **`net_fwd` is 97.9% (15.344s of 15.7s d=14 cell)**, np_unique_loop is 0.9%. Vectorizing the np.unique loop has an upper bound of ~1s out of 112s — **the np.unique vectorization plan was dead before it started**. The actual lever is reducing what we ask the net to score.
- **C4.5 + C4.6 landed (`ef6a909` early-exit, `c410e25` row compaction, `379bf60` ruff fix)** — two compute-reduction commits inserted between C4 and C5.
  - **A1 (early-exit, `ef6a909`):** track `done_mask = (solved_at_step != -1) | (step >= max_steps_per_state)` on-device, sync `.all().item()` once per step, break when nothing left to do. Re-introduces one sync per step (~1-5ms × ~28 steps for d=14 = ~70ms — trivial vs forward savings). Bit-exact equivalence vs C4.
  - **A2 (row compaction, `c410e25`):** at each step, `nonzero()` to gather active row indices, run net forward on `(N_active, B, S)` subset, scatter results back. Subtleties handled: backpointers re-keyed per step (positional indexing breaks when rows drop out), `solved_at_step` flushed via scatter before drop-out, n_active==0 break before net call avoids zero-batch edge case. Bit-exact equivalence at all 7×14 cells vs canonical baseline.
  - **A1+A2 perf:** width=128 sniff **111.6s → 72.8s (-35%, finally back to pre-rewrite baseline)**. A1 alone got ~75s, A2 added ~3s. Most of the win came from early-exit; row-compaction has limited room when most rows stay alive through deep cells.
- **CRITICAL FINDING: MPS throughput knee at ~150k states (load-bearing GPU intuition).** The full 7-width sweep (8/16/32/64/128/256/512) at the new HEAD took **2003s = 33 min vs the canonical 8 min baseline — 4× SLOWER overall, driven entirely by width=256 (663s vs 91s, 7×) and width=512 (1200s vs 156s, 7.7×).** Mechanism: C1's cross-scramble batching makes the forward pass `N × beam_width × 12` = 307k states at width=256, 614k at width=512. **MPS throughput peaks at ~150-200k states for this 5120/1024 ValueNet, then crashes.** Pre-rewrite ran each scramble's beam independently at `beam_width × 12` = 3-6k states — well below the knee. **The cross-scramble batching architecture (C1's foundation) is counterproductive at high beam widths.** The plan's mental model — "bigger batch = better kernel-launch amortization" — was correct only below the knee.
- **State after A1+A2:** width=128 (production default) is back to baseline parity. Width=256 (production default for cycle decisions per prior block!) is **7× slower than baseline** — would make C5's canonical sweep land at ~33 min, embarrassing. Width=128 d=14 cell wall is ~16s vs the plan's ≤15s gate — basically meeting the per-cell gate at production default.
- **Pivoted: BF16 inference experiment (2 commits — `a50c08c`, `9325cc4`).** After the perf math (net_fwd is 98% of width=128 wall, ≤15s/cell mathematically unreachable from architecture changes), reframed the problem as "reduce FLOPs/state, not orchestrate Python better". One-line `network.py` change (one_hot cast follows `self.fc_in.weight.dtype` instead of forcing `.float()`) plus `--precision {fp32,bf16,fp16}` flag in `beam_eval_run.py`. Validated experiment at `experiments/precision-sweep/`: solve rates **bit-identical** at all 14 depths × 7 widths × 3 precisions; wall reduction **1.37× at width=128, 5.13× at width=256, 1.03× at width=512.** **Width=256 BF16 fixes most of the cross-batch regression** — MPS throughput knee shifts from ~150k states at FP32 to ~300k at BF16. Saved a project memory at `project_mps_throughput_knee_m4_max.md`. Test count `560 → 572`.
- **BF16 default flipped (`3862031`)** — `scripts/beam_eval_run.py` and `scripts/post_run_beam_eval.py` now default to bf16. Pass `--precision fp32` to reproduce pre-flip behavior. Training untouched. Confirmed via fresh width=256 sweep: 91.5s wall, 0.72 solve_rate@d=14 (matches FP32 baseline).
- **Per-cell timing decomposition (post-BF16 width=256 d=1..14):** total ~84s, with d=14 alone ~15s (18%), deep tail d=11..14 ~45s (53%), shallow d=1..5 only ~9s (11%). Throughput drops from ~800k states/s at mid depths to 562k/s at d=14 due to row-compaction overhead at the deep frontier.
- **Beam-eval CLI refactored (2 commits — `cd47c7e`, `ca69ce2`).** Reshaped the user-facing tooling: positional `<model.pt>` instead of `--checkpoint <path>`, YAML configs (`fast`, `default`) bundle the per-depth sample schedule + beam_width + precision + seed. CLI overrides win over YAML. `n_per_depth` becomes a sequence (length determines max walk depth; `--max-depth N` slices). File renames via `git mv` (history preserved): `beam_eval_run.py → beam_eval_model.py` (single checkpoint), `post_run_beam_eval.py → beam_eval_run.py` (run dir — "run" now unambiguously means training-run-dir). New `beam_eval_sweep.py` for parameter sweeps over `width|precision|seed|max-depth`. Renderer auto-detects new flat schema vs legacy width-keyed schema. **No backwards compat for trajectory rollup** — per-checkpoint JSONs only at `<run-dir>/results/<step>_eval_<config>.json`. Configs at `scripts/eval-configs/{fast,default}.yaml`. Per-depth sample counts picked from binomial SE math: fast targets SE~6.5pp at deep tail (n=50), default targets SE~4.6pp (n=100), shallow saturated depths use minimum-meaningful n. Test count `572 → 615` (+43).
- **Validation:**
  - `beam_eval_model.py <full_train net_final.pt> --config fast`: 39.3s wall, solve@d=14=0.52 (n=50)
  - `beam_eval_model.py <full_train net_final.pt> --config default`: 89.7s wall, solve@d=14=0.67 (n=100)
  - Disagreement at d=14 is 15pp — within 2σ given the binomial SE difference (combined 8.5pp, 2σ=17pp). Per-depth reseeding means each depth's walk distribution is fixed; n change scales only its own sample count without perturbing other depths.
  - `beam_eval_sweep.py <model> --over precision --values "fp32,bf16" --config fast` produces both expected files with `_precision=fp32.json` and `_precision=bf16.json` suffixes; solve rates bit-equal as expected.
- **`--render-html` flag wired** (`062e478` initial CLI flag, then `8b5189d` moved render_html into the YAML config bundle so behavior is config-driven; CLI `--render-html` / `--no-render-html` overrides). `fast.yaml` defaults `render_html: false` (just numbers, fast cycling); `default.yaml` defaults `true` (verdict tier wants the visualization). For `beam_eval_run.py`, the YAML field gates a **single overlay HTML across all per-checkpoint JSONs**, not per-checkpoint files. Test count `615 → 626` over the two commits.
- **Trajectory renderer rewrite (`3c069da`).** First render of warm_continue was a mess — long filenames in legends, heatmap Y-axis upside-down, per-walk-depth lines instead of per-step trajectories. Per the project's banded small-multiples convention (CLAUDE.md, `render_error_trajectories.py`), trajectory mode now: (a) detects multi-input + varying `step` field, (b) renders 5/5/4 banded per-walk-depth small multiples with X=step / Y=solve_rate, (c) heatmap Y-axis reversed (d=14 at top — that's where the interesting collapse lives), (d) uses JSON's `step` field for legend labels with "Nk" formatting. Sweep mode (varying width / precision) keeps the legacy layout but with cleaner labels. Test count `626 → 631`.
- **End-to-end validation: warm_continue trajectory eval.** `beam_eval_run.py <run-dir> --config fast --every-steps 10000` produced 12 per-checkpoint JSONs in ~8 min. Re-rendered HTML at `<run-dir>/results/trajectory_fast.html`. Reproduced the prior block's collapse curve: peak capability at step 40k (+24k post-warm, d=14=0.60), monotonic erosion to step 120k (d=14=0.12). Slightly noisier than prior block's measurement (n=50 vs n=100) but within 2σ binomial SE — same shape, same story.

**Outcome:**
- **Block goal partially met, with a major reframe.** Original perf gate (≤15s/cell, ≤2 min total sweep) was mathematically unreachable from architectural changes — the per-cell wall is 98% net forward, and on M4 Max we're already at ~60% of theoretical FP32 peak. The plan attacked the wrong bottleneck. **The actual perf lever was inference precision.** BF16 inference (a 10-line code change) delivered 1.37× at width=128 and 5.13× at width=256 with bit-identical solve rates. That single experiment justified the whole block.
- **What landed (16 commits on `beam-solve-perf` ahead of main):**
  - Cross-scramble batched beam search (C1-C4 + A1+A2): bit-exact equivalence preserved, perf-neutral at production widths after BF16 corrects the throughput-knee regression. Cleaner architecture and per-state `max_steps_per_state` API as a side benefit.
  - `src/rubik/search/state_hash.py`: FNV-1a 64-bit cube state hash with documented MPS int64 unique/sort bug workaround. Reusable for future search/dedup work.
  - BF16 inference path with `--precision {fp32,bf16,fp16}` flag, BF16 as production default. `experiments/precision-sweep/` writeup with results.md + intuition.md.
  - Beam-eval CLI refactor: `beam_eval_model.py <model.pt> --config NAME` (positional model, YAML-driven), `beam_eval_run.py <run-dir>` (run-dir wrapper with `--every-steps`), `beam_eval_sweep.py <model.pt> --over PARAM` (parameter sweeps), with `render_html` as a YAML config field driving auto-render of overlay HTMLs. `scripts/eval-configs/{fast,default}.yaml` with binomial-SE-justified sample schedules.
  - Trajectory-mode renderer following project's 5/5/4 banded small-multiples convention.
- **Decision recorded: BF16 is the production inference default for 3x3.** Bit-identical solve rates on the trained net (zero topk ties), 1.37× wall at width=128, 5.13× at width=256. Training stays FP32 (gradient stability concerns there are real). Disable per-call with `--precision fp32` if needed for bit-exact reproduction.
- **Decision recorded: cross-scramble batching architecture stays.** It's perf-neutral at production widths (width=128 at baseline parity, width=256 within 1.24× of pre-rewrite baseline thanks to BF16). The width=512 regression (5× slower than per-scramble baseline) is acceptable since width=512 is diagnostic-only per the prior block. Reverting to recover width=512 perf isn't worth the lost architecture and reusable infra. Chunked-forward fix is a documented followup if/when it matters.
- **Decision recorded: `n_per_depth` is a per-depth sequence picked from binomial SE math, not a uniform scalar.** d=1 has only 12 reachable walks — n=12 is exhaustive. Shallow d=2..6 saturate at p=1, low-n is fine. Deep d=7..14 are SE-bound. `fast` config targets SE~6.5pp at deep tail (n=50, ~40s/checkpoint at width=256 BF16). `default` config targets SE~4.6pp (n=100, ~90s/checkpoint).
- **Load-bearing GPU intuition captured (saved as project memory `project_mps_throughput_knee_m4_max.md`):** M4 Max MPS throughput is roughly flat for the [5120, 1024]×4 ValueNet from batch=1.5k → ~150-200k states, then crashes hard above. BF16 shifts the knee to ~300k states. "Bigger batch = better via kernel amortization" is true only below the knee. This inverted the plan's mental model and explains why width=256 cross-batch is fast under BF16 but slow under FP32.
- **Load-bearing platform bug documented in `state_hash.py` + `beam.py`:** `torch.unique` and `torch.sort` on int64 tensors on MPS only consider the **low 32 bits**. Bisected via small-N solve sweep + direct probe. Workaround: keep hash data in low 32 bits OR fall back to CPU dedup. Will re-bite us elsewhere if forgotten.
- **Honest cost picture for evals (post-block):**
  - Screening (`fast` profile, width=256 BF16): ~40s for full d=1..14 on one checkpoint, ~8 min for a 12-checkpoint trajectory.
  - Production cycle decision (`default` profile, width=256 BF16): ~90s for one checkpoint, ~18 min for a 12-checkpoint trajectory.
  - Diagnostic (`default` + width=512): ~13 min for one checkpoint at width=512 (the chunked-forward followup would help here).
- **Followups (notes for next block / backlog — see ROADMAP.md):**
  - **Checkpoint metadata bundle.** Today eval scripts parse step from filenames (`net_step_26000.pt`) and resolve sibling `config.yaml` for arch. Fragile. Bundle `{net_state, step, config_snapshot, training_metadata}` directly into the .pt instead. Fixes: parameter values that change across training (K_max ramp, LR schedule) become recoverable from a bare checkpoint; no filename parsing.
  - **Chunked forward at high beam widths.** Width=512 still ~5× slower than per-scramble baseline. Wrap `net(...)` with a chunking helper at ≤150k states.
  - **Multi-run trajectory heatmap layout.** Renderer's heatmap section currently shows only the first run's matrix when multiple runs are passed. Banded line chart and walltime line do overlay correctly. Cycle-vs-cycle trajectory comparisons would benefit.
  - **T0 capacity sweep on 3x3.** Pre-existing backlog item, possibly the biggest perf lever left — net forward is 98% of wall, smaller net = direct speedup. Stub at `experiments/davi-3x3/calibrate_step_time_3x3.py`.
  - **K=8 V* oracle expansion** (pre-existing backlog).
- **Drive-by:** none specific to this block — the BF16 experiment, beam-eval refactor, and trajectory renderer were all originally drive-bys but ended up large enough to justify their own commits-bundles inside the block.

**Commits:** `8d6c77b` (open), `a5e74c1` (plan), `c0b293e` (C1 cross-scramble batch), `2d0ee4e` (C2 run-to-completion), `2ec4a5d` (C3 on-device hash dedup), `95892a7` (C4 torch.topk), `ef6a909` (A1 early-exit), `c410e25` (A2 row compaction), `379bf60` (ruff fix), `a50c08c` (BF16 code change), `9325cc4` (precision-sweep experiment), `3862031` (BF16 default flip), `cd47c7e` (refactor pt 1: renames + YAML), `ca69ce2` (refactor pt 2: run-dir + sweep), `26b1fde` (LOG amend), `062e478` (--render-html flag), `8b5189d` (render_html into YAML), `3c069da` (trajectory renderer), `9abdb66` (close + outcome), `bcc6306` (warm_continue eval artifacts). 20 commits, branch `beam-solve-perf` ahead of main.

## 2026-05-07 — Beam-eval as primary metric: parameterized tool + dedicated HTML + width sweep ✅ done
**Goal:** Promote beam-eval from "one analysis pass among many" to **the primary 3x3 capability metric**, with first-class tooling. Today the only beam-eval invocation path is `scripts/post_run_beam_eval.py --run-dir <path>` (tied to a run directory's checkpoints), and its output is rendered as dodged bars at the bottom of `experiments/davi-3x3/results/error_trajectories.html` (mixed with macro / per-V*-MAE / pred-trajectory plots — error metrics, not capability). The framing is wrong: beam-eval is what tells us whether a checkpoint can solve cubes, full stop. It deserves a parameterized CLI that points at any model and a dedicated HTML report. Three deliverables in this block: (1) parameterized CLI that takes `--checkpoint`, `--widths` (csv sweep), `--max-walk-depth`, `--n-per-depth`, `--device`, `--output-json`, plus per-width wall-time timing; (2) dedicated HTML renderer that consumes the JSON and shows per-walk-depth solve rate × width with timing overlay — decoupled from any run dir; (3) remove the beam-capability sections from `error_trajectories.html` so the trajectories report stays scoped to *errors* (calibration / V*-MAE / pred drift). First execution of the new tool: sweep `beam_width ∈ {8, 16, 32, 64, 128, 256, 512}` on `full_train net_final.pt` (our d=14=0.78 baseline at step 16k) to find the speed/quality tradeoff and pick a faster routine-analysis default. Today's beam-eval at width=256 takes ~5 min per checkpoint; if width=64 (or smaller) holds capability within ~3pp at d=14, that's a 4x throughput win for everything downstream.
**Milestone:** M8 phase 3 tooling + drive-by infrastructure
**Approach:** Branch `m8-beam-eval-tooling` from main HEAD `65626ec`. Five sub-steps:
- **Step 1 (design first, code second):** Nail down the CLI surface for the new tool — likely `scripts/beam_eval_run.py` — and the JSON output schema. Reuses `beam_eval_walk` (and optionally `beam_eval_v_star`) from `experiments/davi-3x3/eval.py`; the script is mostly orchestration + width-sweep loop + timing. Schema sketch: top-level `{"checkpoint": "<path>", "device": "...", "max_walk_depth": N, "n_per_depth": N, "results": {"<width>": {"per_walk_depth": [{"d": N, "solve_rate": f, "avg_solve_len": f, "n": N}, ...], "wall_time_seconds": f, "states_scored": N (optional)}}}`. Discuss schema before writing.
- **Step 2:** Build `scripts/beam_eval_run.py` — argparse CLI, loads checkpoint into a fresh `ValueNet` (arch from a passed-in config, or auto-inferred from state_dict shape if feasible), runs `beam_eval_walk` once per width, captures wall time per width, writes the JSON output. Tests for: schema correctness, timing fields populated, deterministic-from-seed (same seed → same solve rates within float epsilon). Optional follow-up: `--include-v-star` flag for the V*-stratified eval too.
- **Step 3:** Build `scripts/render_beam_eval_report.py` — consumes one or more beam-eval JSONs and produces a dedicated HTML report. Charts: per-walk-depth solve rate (one line per width), wall-time-per-width bar chart, optionally per-walk-depth × per-width heatmap. Renders to a configurable output path (default e.g. `<json-stem>.html` next to the input). Tests minimal — visual artifact.
- **Step 4:** Remove beam-capability sections from `experiments/davi-3x3/analysis/render_error_trajectories.py`. Confirm `analyze.py` doesn't need changes (it consumes per-eval `metrics.jsonl` records, not beam JSONs). Regenerate `error_trajectories.html` to confirm it's now scoped purely to errors.
- **Step 5:** First execution of the new tooling: sweep widths {8, 16, 32, 64, 128, 256, 512} on `experiments/davi-3x3/runs/20260507T043533Z_full_train/net_final.pt` (best baseline). Estimated wall: roughly the time to run beam_eval_walk 7 times — call it 30-60 min total, with smaller widths dramatically faster. Results land at `experiments/beam-eval-perf/` per the project's perf-experiment-loop convention. Writeup `experiments/beam-eval-perf/results.md` + hand-written `intuition.md` per project convention; pick a production default and document the gate.
**Next:** Block closed. Merging to main. Headline: width=256 stays as production default — width=64 / 128 sacrifice too much d=14 capability for the throughput they buy.
**In progress:**
- Block opened. Branch `m8-beam-eval-tooling` from main HEAD `65626ec`.
- **Steps 1-4 (tooling) landed across 3 commits via subagent.** Test count `553 → 560` (7 new tests in `tests/scripts/`). `error_trajectories.html` reduced 279 → 216 KB; `grep -ic beam <html>` → 0 matches. Schema decision: `--checkpoint` auto-discovers sibling `config.yaml` from the run dir, falling back to explicit `--config`. Output JSON is `{checkpoint, config_path, device, max_walk_depth, n_per_depth, seed, results: {<width>: {per_walk_depth, wall_time_seconds, states_scored}}}` with optional `v_star_results` under `--include-v-star` (skipped for this first run since V*=1..6 saturates on the current 3x3 model).
- **Step 5 width sweep on `full_train/net_final.pt`** (widths {8,16,32,64,128,256,512}, n_per_depth=100, seed=0): **total wall ~8 min** — a third of the 30-60 min estimate, since smaller widths share scheduling overhead and only width=256 / 512 dominate. Per-walk-depth × per-width matrix:

  | width | wall (s) | d=10 | d=11 | d=12 | d=13 | d=14 |
  |---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
  | 8   |  30.6 | 0.90 | 0.83 | 0.63 | 0.29 | 0.19 |
  | 16  |  30.6 | 0.97 | 0.88 | 0.73 | 0.44 | 0.26 |
  | 32  |  48.7 | 0.99 | 0.96 | 0.82 | 0.58 | 0.40 |
  | 64  |  65.5 | 1.00 | 0.99 | 0.92 | 0.70 | 0.49 |
  | 128 |  69.1 | 1.00 | 1.00 | 0.97 | 0.82 | 0.64 |
  | 256 |  90.8 | 1.00 | 1.00 | 0.99 | 0.86 | 0.71 |
  | 512 | 156.1 | 1.00 | 1.00 | 0.99 | 0.93 | 0.79 |

- **Headline finding (block hypothesis falsified):** "width=64 holds capability within ~3pp of width=256 → 4× throughput win" did not hold. Width=64 trails width=256 by **22pp at d=14** (0.49 vs 0.71). Even width=128 trails by 7pp. The d=14 capability is genuinely beam-bound on this checkpoint — there's no free fast default.
- **Surprise: width=64 → 128 is nearly free** (1.05× wall, +15pp at d=14 — the single most efficient doubling in the sweep). Likely a hardware sweet spot: beam_width × n_moves = 128 × 12 = 1536 fits one MPS launch where 64 × 12 = 768 leaves SIMD lanes idle. Means width=128 dominates width=64 on the M4 Max — never a reason to pick width=64 when width=128 costs 4 extra seconds.
- **Production default decision:** keep `width=256` as the production default for cycle-to-cycle decisions. Three operational tiers documented in `experiments/beam-eval-perf/results.md`:
  - **Screening** (66s/checkpoint): width=64 — for "is this trained at all" sanity checks
  - **Production** (91s/checkpoint): width=256 — for cycle decisions
  - **Deep diagnostic** (156s/checkpoint): width=512 — for settling tied verdicts at d=14
- **Pre-committed gate `d=14 ≥ 0.75`** met only by width=512 on this seed (0.79). Width=256 here is 0.71, vs the prior 0.78 baseline at the same checkpoint — 1.5σ within walk-sample noise (binomial SE = 0.046 at p=0.7, n=100). Treated as walk-variance, not regression. Width=512 matches the prior 0.79 baseline within 1pp — the higher-width cell is more stable across seeds.

**Outcome:**
- **All five planned steps shipped in 5 atomic commits on `m8-beam-eval-tooling`.** Branch merges back to main.
- **Tooling delta:** new parameterized beam-eval CLI (`scripts/beam_eval_run.py`) and dedicated HTML renderer (`scripts/render_beam_eval_report.py`) — `--checkpoint` auto-discovers sibling `config.yaml`, sweeps any user-specified width csv, supports `--include-v-star`, deterministic from `--seed`. Test count `553 → 560`. Old beam-capability sections deleted from `experiments/davi-3x3/analysis/render_error_trajectories.py`; that report now scoped purely to error/calibration metrics (216 KB, no beam content). `scripts/post_run_beam_eval.py` left alone — it remains the trajectory-eval-during-training pathway, complementary to the new ad-hoc tool.
- **First experiment landed:** `experiments/beam-eval-perf/` with `results.md` + hand-written `intuition.md` + raw JSON + dedicated HTML, following the M4 perf-experiment-loop convention.
- **Decision recorded: the new tool's default workflow is `point at a checkpoint, get a sweep`.** No need to be inside a run dir, no need to know what `--config` is — auto-discovery handles the common case. The CLI sweep replaces "edit the script and re-run" as the way to ask perf questions about specific checkpoints.
- **Decision recorded: the production beam_width for 3x3 cycle decisions stays at 256.** Reasoning above. The "fast default" hypothesis that motivated this experiment is falsified for the current 3x3 model — d=14 is genuinely beam-bound and a smaller default would hide signal.
- **Decision recorded: width=128 is strictly preferred over width=64 on M4 Max** for any case where you'd consider width=64. The 5% wall premium (4 extra seconds on this sweep) buys +15pp at d=14 — there's no operational point in choosing width=64 over width=128 unless you're explicitly testing the lower-width regime.
- **Decision recorded: width=512 is reserved for diagnostic questions, not production.** +8pp at 1.72× wall is real but expensive; we'd burn ~5 hours of wall over the typical 100+ checkpoint trajectory if we promoted it to default.
- **Surprise (worth investigating later):** the width=8 vs width=16 wall-time tie. Both at 30.6s. Implies a fixed scheduling overhead floor under which doubling work is free. Same kind of regime as the 64→128 sweet spot, at the small end. Suggests M4 Max has *two* batched-MPS efficiency knees in the beam_width × n_moves space — at the small end (overhead-dominated, small batches all cost the same) and around 128 (next launch boundary). Pure speculation; H1 in `intuition.md` proposes a verification path.
- **Followups (notes for next block / backlog):**
  - **Multi-seed CI** for the d=14 cells. n=100 binomial SE is wide enough that 1-cell verdicts are noisy. 10-seed re-run would tighten the ±5pp bands on this sweep into proper 95% CIs. ~80 min wall.
  - **Sweep on a known-bad checkpoint** (warm_continue/net_step_120000.pt, d=14=0.24 baseline). Tells us whether the saturation knees move with checkpoint quality or are universal — H3 in intuition.md.
  - **Extend to widths {1024, 2048}** — does d=14 climb past 0.79 or saturate? ~12 min wall additional. H4 in intuition.md.
  - **Tooling improvement:** thread the actual node count through `beam_eval_walk` so `states_scored` reflects real beam expansions, not just root-state count.
  - Pending from prior blocks: K=8 V* oracle expansion, full resumability infra, smaller_net comparison run.
- **Drive-by:** none.
**Commits:** `24d072b` (block open), `0b6cc2b` (Step 2 — beam_eval_run.py + tests), `2bea87d` (Step 3 — render_beam_eval_report.py + tests), `c2a6813` (Step 4 — strip beam from error_trajectories.py), `21776bd` (Step 5 — sweep + writeup), `<this commit>` (Outcome).

## 2026-05-07 — Diagnostic: dense beam-eval over warm_continue collapse window ✅ done
**Goal:** Resolve the open question from the prior block — *when* did capability begin eroding in the warm_continue run? The prior block beam-evaled at steps 20k/30k/50k/70k/90k/110k/120k. Step 20k (4k post-warm-start) was already at d=14=0.64 (down 14pp from full_train's 0.78). We don't yet know if the regression hit in the first 2k post-warm steps (Adam moments thrashing → catastrophic single-window destabilization) or built gradually across the early window. Beam-eval at steps 18k, 22k, 24k, 26k, 28k fills in the gap. Cheap (~25 min wall), self-contained, no codebase changes.
**Milestone:** drive-by diagnostic following `1b1727f` (M8 phase 3)
**Approach:** Branch `m8-warm-collapse-diagnostic` from main HEAD `1b1727f`. Run `scripts/post_run_beam_eval.py --checkpoint-steps "18000,22000,24000,26000,28000"` against the warm_continue run dir. The `--checkpoint-steps` flag's merge-mode appends to the existing trajectory JSON. Regenerate the analysis HTML. Pull the trajectory and judge.
**Next:** Block closed; merging to main. Findings refine the prior block's "Adam thrashing" hypothesis.
**In progress:**
- Block opened. Branch `m8-warm-collapse-diagnostic` from main HEAD `1b1727f`.
- **Beam-eval landed cleanly** (~25 min wall). Trajectory JSON merged via `--checkpoint-steps` accumulation logic; now contains 12 datapoints (steps 18k/20k/22k/24k/26k/28k/30k/50k/70k/90k/110k/120k).
- **Full deep-tail trajectory at beam_width=256 (now with collapse-window detail in bold):**

  | step | d=10 | d=11 | d=12 | d=13 | d=14 |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | full_train 16k (warm-start source) | 1.000 | 1.000 | 0.990 | 0.910 | 0.780 |
  | **18k (+2k)** | **1.000** | **1.000** | **0.980** | **0.860** | **0.750** |
  | 20k (+4k) | 1.000 | 1.000 | 0.980 | 0.900 | 0.640 |
  | **22k (+6k)** | **1.000** | **1.000** | **0.990** | **0.850** | **0.650** |
  | **24k (+8k)** | **1.000** | **0.990** | **0.990** | **0.860** | **0.690** |
  | **26k (+10k)** | **1.000** | **1.000** | **0.990** | **0.900** | **0.700** |
  | **28k (+12k)** | **1.000** | **1.000** | **1.000** | **0.920** | **0.620** |
  | 30k (+14k) | 1.000 | 0.990 | 0.990 | 0.930 | 0.640 |
  | 50k | 1.000 | 1.000 | 1.000 | 0.850 | 0.580 |
  | 70k | 1.000 | 1.000 | 0.980 | 0.830 | 0.480 |
  | 90k | 1.000 | 1.000 | 0.920 | 0.690 | 0.420 |
  | 110k | 1.000 | 0.980 | 0.840 | 0.470 | 0.230 |
  | 120k | 0.990 | 0.970 | 0.790 | 0.390 | 0.240 |

- HTML regenerated at `experiments/davi-3x3/results/error_trajectories.html` (279 KB).

**Outcome:**
- **Headline: the original "Adam thrashing in the first 2k steps" hypothesis is REFUTED.** Step 18k (just 2k post-warm-start) shows d=14=0.75 — only 3pp below the baseline 0.78. d=13=0.86 (vs 0.91 baseline). Capability is *preserved* in the immediate post-warm-start window. The fresh-Adam + target_net=copy-of-model design distortion did NOT cause an instant catastrophe.
- **Refined picture: collapse is a SLOW gradual drift that ACCELERATES with more training.** Three regimes visible in the trajectory:
  1. **Steps 16k → 30k (early window, +14k steps):** d=14 oscillates in [0.62, 0.75] — within plausible n=100 eval-set variance (std error ~5pp at solve rate ≈ 0.7). Capability roughly preserved. d=13 actually peaks *above* baseline at step 30k (0.93 vs 0.91).
  2. **Steps 30k → 70k (mid window, +40k steps):** slow erosion. d=14: 0.64 → 0.48. d=13: 0.93 → 0.83. About −0.4pp/1k steps at d=14.
  3. **Steps 70k → 120k (late window, +50k steps):** accelerated collapse. d=14: 0.48 → 0.24. d=13: 0.83 → 0.39. About −0.5pp/1k steps at d=14, but the d=13 collapse is the dramatic one (−44pp in 50k steps).
- **The acceleration in the late window suggests a phase transition, not steady-state drift.** Linear erosion would give a roughly constant rate; instead we see steepening. Plausibly the network is approaching some attractor where deep-tail ordering becomes unstable, and once the slope exceeds some threshold, the collapse compounds. Hypothesis (unverified): the Bellman bootstrap target itself drifts as the network drifts, creating a positive feedback loop that grows with training time. Confirming this would need: track per-V*-MAE drift across the same windows, and/or measure the L2 distance between value_net and target_net over training.
- **Mechanism implication: it's NOT (just) the warm-start design distortion.** The first 2k post-warm steps preserved capability; the long erosion is what hurts. This means even a *full* resumability infra (preserved Adam moments + preserved target_net lag) likely wouldn't fully solve the problem if the underlying issue is "DAVI on a near-converged net at the same K_max drifts toward a worse attractor over long training." Resumability infra is still valuable (and still planned), but it may not be the silver bullet for the warm-continue regression.
- **Decision recorded: the "warm-continue at the same K_max" experimental design itself appears to be the issue, not (just) the warm-start mechanics.** Possible better approaches to test next:
  - Warm-continue with K_max RAISED (e.g. 16k checkpoint → continue at K_max=25 or 30) — the network would face a new training distribution, plausibly avoiding the drift-toward-degenerate-attractor failure mode
  - Warm-continue with a higher learning rate decay schedule — slow the drift
  - Cycle short bursts of training rather than one long run — re-evaluate beam capability mid-run, save the best-by-beam-eval checkpoint
- **Decision recorded: the original full_train trajectory's d=14=0.78 at step 16k may be CLOSE TO A LOCAL MAXIMUM for K_max=20.** Continuing to train at K=20 from there appears to slowly (then quickly) erode it. To push past it, we likely need a curriculum bump (raise K_max), not just more steps.
- **Decision recorded: full resumability infra remains a priority** but its scope is now framed as "needed to preserve training continuity for short multi-cycle runs," NOT "needed to fix the warm_continue collapse." The collapse is mostly a long-training-trajectory-at-fixed-K_max phenomenon, not an Adam-state phenomenon.
- **Followups (notes for next block / backlog):**
  - **Diagnostic: track L2 distance between value_net and target_net across the warm_continue training.** If the distance grows monotonically over the 100k steps and accelerates in the late window, that's evidence for the "feedback loop drift" mechanism. Cheap to compute post-hoc from saved checkpoints (the run dir has all 52 of them).
  - **Diagnostic: per-V*-MAE trajectory at higher V*=k (k>6) using random-walk states with known V* upper bound**. Hard without the full V* oracle, but for V*≤6 we have the bounded oracle; we can reuse it to track whether shallow MAE drifts in lockstep with deep capability.
  - **Next training experiment (HIGH-VALUE per the new analysis):** warm-continue from full_train net_final.pt with K_max raised to 25 or 30. Tests the "raise K_max to escape the local max" hypothesis. Single overnight run.
  - **Resumability infra still on the bench** for the multi-cycle short-burst pattern.
  - K=8 oracle expansion (existing backlog, priority unchanged).
- **Drive-by:** none on this block. Tightly scoped diagnostic.
**Commits:** `b981b59` (block open), `<this commit>` (Outcome).

## 2026-05-06 — Instrumentation cleanup + warm-start overnight rerun ✅ done — capability collapse
**Goal:** Land four instrumentation fixes that emerged from today's full_train run, then kick off an overnight rerun that **warm-starts from today's `net_final.pt`** (step 16,000) and continues training. The full_train beam-eval revealed deep-d capability climbing monotonically through what `macro_v_star_mae` called a "regression" — d=14 went 0.59 → 0.78 across the steps macro called bad. Warm-starting from that checkpoint and running another ~100k steps tests whether the climb continues, plateaus, or inverts — the exact question early-stop terminated before answering. Same arch as today (`[5120, 1024] × 4 BN`), K_max=20 unchanged, no early-stop. Instrumentation fixes: (1) wandb panel namespacing into ~6 sections instead of one giant `eval/` blob, (2) zero-pad `dN` keys to `dNN` so wandb sorts them naturally instead of `d1, d10, d11, d12, d2, d3, ...`, (3) `wandb.define_metric("*", step_metric="step")` so future panels default to training-step on x-axis instead of wandb's `_step` log-call counter, (4) banded aggregate metrics (shallow d=1..5 / mid d=6..10 / deep d=11..14 means) alongside per-d keys for at-a-glance trend reads. Plus remove early-stop wiring (kept config fields for backwards compat).
**Milestone:** drive-by + M8 phase 3 ([plan](plans/m8-3x3-davi.md))
**Approach:** Branch `m8-instrumentation-cleanup` from main HEAD `da50495`. Four atomic commits: (A) wandb cleanup in `src/rubik/training/wandb_sink.py` + tests + workspace doc, (B) remove early-stop wiring in `experiments/davi-3x3/run.py` + define_metric call, (C) new config `experiments/davi-3x3/configs/smaller_net.yaml` (retained for a future comparison evening, NOT used tonight), (D) minimal `--warm-start` + `--from-step` CLI flags in `run.py` + new config `experiments/davi-3x3/configs/warm_continue.yaml` + integration test. The warm-start is deliberately scoped narrow — load model state_dict, fresh optimizer (Adam moments NOT preserved; ~500 steps to re-warm acknowledged), target_net = copy of model. Full resumability infrastructure (optimizer state + target_net state + wandb run continuity) is a separate block tomorrow.
**Next:** Block closed. Capability collapse is the headline finding. Next block depends on the morning conversation — likely "land full resumability infra (preserve Adam moments + target_net state) before any further long warm-continued runs."
**In progress:**
- Block opened. Branch `m8-instrumentation-cleanup` from main HEAD `da50495`.
- **Change of plan after A/B/C landed:** original tonight-config was a smaller-net-from-scratch (`smaller_net.yaml`, [2048, 1024] × 4 BN, 50k steps). After seeing today's beam-eval results — deep-d climbing monotonically through the macro's claimed regression — user pivoted to warm-start-from-current-net to test whether the d=14 climb continues past the step-16k early-stop point. `smaller_net.yaml` retained as a future comparison config for a different evening (not deleted; not used tonight). Adding D commit on top of A/B/C.
- **6 commits landed pre-launch (subagent):** `1bd2324` (block open), `c616440` (A — wandb namespacing into 6 sections + zero-pad d-keys + banded shallow/mid/deep aggregates in `value_eval` returned dict + workspace doc), `57efb5f` (B — early-stop wiring removed; `wandb.define_metric("*", step_metric="step")` added so panels default to training-step), `d1b109e` (C — `smaller_net.yaml` retained), `53a5236` (D1 — `--warm-start <ckpt>` + `--from-step N` flags in `run.py` with model state_dict load, target_net = copy of model, fresh Adam optimizer, step counter init from arg, `event="run_start"` annotated with `warm_start_from` + `from_step`; 8 new tests), `f154d7a` (D2 — `warm_continue.yaml`: arch unchanged, `n_steps=120000`, `checkpoint_every=2000`, `early_stop_enabled=false`). Test count **545 → 553**. Subagent ran a warm-start smoke against the actual `net_final.pt` (50 training steps in /tmp; loss trajectory consistent with the warm-start docstring's ~500-step optimizer-rewarming distortion). Smoke artifacts cleaned up.
- **Overnight warm-start run launched (`bqynb1lhi`) and completed cleanly.** Command: `uv run python experiments/davi-3x3/run.py --config experiments/davi-3x3/configs/warm_continue.yaml --warm-start experiments/davi-3x3/runs/20260507T043533Z_full_train/net_final.pt --from-step 16000`. Run dir `experiments/davi-3x3/runs/20260507T064517Z_warm_continue/`. wandb: https://wandb.ai/griffin-enterprises/rubik-3x3/runs/sw6i2daz. ~7.3h wall, full 120k steps, no NaN, no MPS errors. macro_v_star_mae trajectory plateaued in **0.64-0.78 band** with slow downward drift (final 0.6662). `d=14 pred_mean` descended **8.35 → 7.60** and `d=14 pred_std` tightened **0.85 → 0.68** over the run — the network became more confident in lower predictions for deep walks.

Wall ~30 min. Results landed in `runs/20260507T064517Z_warm_continue/results/post_run_beam_eval.json`.
- **Headline: capability COLLAPSED through warm-continue training.** Per-walk-depth solve rate at the deep tail, beam_width=256:

  | step | d=10 | d=11 | d=12 | d=13 | d=14 |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | full_train 16k (warm-start source) | 1.000 | 1.000 | **0.990** | **0.910** | **0.780** |
  | warm_continue 20k (4k post-warm) | 1.000 | 1.000 | 0.980 | 0.900 | 0.640 |
  | warm_continue 30k | 1.000 | 0.990 | 0.990 | 0.930 | 0.640 |
  | warm_continue 50k | 1.000 | 1.000 | 1.000 | 0.850 | 0.580 |
  | warm_continue 70k | 1.000 | 1.000 | 0.980 | 0.830 | 0.480 |
  | warm_continue 90k | 1.000 | 1.000 | 0.920 | 0.690 | 0.420 |
  | warm_continue 110k | 1.000 | 0.980 | 0.840 | 0.470 | 0.230 |
  | warm_continue 120k (final) | 0.990 | 0.970 | 0.790 | 0.390 | **0.240** |

  d=14: 0.78 → 0.24 (**−54pp**). d=13: 0.91 → 0.39 (**−52pp**). d=12: 0.99 → 0.79. d=11: 1.00 → 0.97. d=10: 1.00 → 0.99. The deeper the tail, the larger the regression — and the regression accelerated late (step 90k → 120k saw d=14 drop from 0.42 → 0.24).
- **macro_v_star_mae again missed the regression entirely** — sat in 0.64-0.78 throughout, while beam capability at d=14 collapsed by 54pp. Per-V*-MAE on V*=1..6 (which macro averages) was roughly flat across the run (V*=6 MAE drifted from 1.35 → 1.42, slightly *worse*). The network's V*=1..6 region stayed stable; the deep-walk region eroded silently.
- **Analysis pipeline regenerated.** `experiments/davi-3x3/results/error_trajectories.html` now overlays all three runs (smoke, full_train, warm_continue) at 264 KB. The d=12..14 panels show the warm_continue green line ending well below where full_train's orange line ended.
- **5 commits landed during the morning analysis cycle:** `00acc3f` (`--checkpoint-steps` flag for `post_run_beam_eval.py`), `93fb2a9` (analysis pipeline extended for warm_continue + regenerated HTML), `6fbd25e` (run artifacts: config + metrics + beam_eval JSON), this commit (Outcome).

**Outcome:**
- **Headline: the minimal warm-start design (model weights only; fresh Adam optimizer; target_net = copy of model) catastrophically eroded deep-tail capability over 100k+ steps of continued training.** d=14 went from 0.78 (full_train end / warm-start source) to 0.24 (warm_continue end) — −54pp. d=13: 0.91 → 0.39 (−52pp). d=12: 0.99 → 0.79. The deeper the walk, the worse the regression. The hypothesis "warm-start would let us continue the d=14 climb past full_train's 0.78" is **falsified for this warm-start configuration**.
